#2812. Jordan Davis trade grades: Jaguars-Eagles blockbuster as Jags load up for Super Bowl run

The Jacksonville Jaguars are all in — ALL the way in. On Monday the team completed its third major trade of the day, and its biggest one yet, getting DT Jordan Davis from the Eagles in a truly blockbuster deal.

Jacksonville will send its 1st round pick in 2027, and a 2nd round pick in 2029, according to Adam Schefter, marking a massive overhaul to the Jaguars' defensive line after making a separate deal for Davon Godcheaux of the Saints. This is in addition to the trade they made to kick off Monday, sending a 2nd round pick to the Atlanta Falcons for safety Jessie Bates, who is in the final year of his contract.

This deal marks a massive improvement up front over Ruke Orhorhoro, but it's comes with significant risk too. Jacksonville is pushing all their chips to the center of the table, and they best be right that they're as close to winning a Super Bowl as they think.

Jordan Davis is a solid run-stopping defensive tackle, but isn't a unicorn talent. While he is good at meeting ball carriers at the point of attack, he's a below-average pass rusher and doesn't really meet his opponents downfield. What we're essentially looking at is a Top 15 player at his position as run stopper, and below average against the pass.

This is not the kind of player who is worth a 1st and 2nd round pick unless you're absolutely sure he can be your missing piece. Obviously the Jaguars seem to think so, but the Colts felt the same way when they gave up two first round picks for Sauce Gardner a year ago. Look how that one worked out.

Jacksonville is obviously a better team in 2026 than Indianapolis was a year ago, but the vibes are along the same lines. It's a big, brash move, dripping in confidence, but it's unclear if the team has earned that spot just yet. Davis is designed to help them specifically stop the elite running backs they could face in the playoffs, like James Cook and Kenneth Walker — which is great, but nothing is a foregone conclusion at the moment.

The Jags are currently 6-2, a great spot to be in, but they have played a soft schedule up to this point, where their two losses came against the best competition they faced in Denver and Houston. All six of Jacksonville's wins have come against teams with a combined record of 24-29. Sure, you can only play the teams in front of you — but that could also lead to a miscalculation that you're a lot better than you think you are, and it's here the grade falls a bit.

When we get down to it, trading a 1st and 2nd for Jordan Davis is an overpayment. If this doesn't result in a Super Bowl win, then we'll be talking about this a lot around the 2027 NFL Draft when the Jaguars don't have a 1st round pick for the second straight year because they made a trade.

I am always going to give a big-time writer's tilt when it comes to teams accepting the position they're in. When news first emerged that the Jaguars traded a 1st and 2nd for an Eagles DT we all assumed at SB Nation that Jacksonville was getting Jalen Carter, not Jordan Davis.

Philly gave up a player they can replace and got a significant return. This was a very smart move for a team that doesn't have a shot in 2026 and is playing for next year. Their picks in 2027 can help address their much larger need concerns along the offensive line, and simply improve the team from the bottom up — which needs to happen.

This is an example of an incredibly well-oiled franchise taking advantage of a team desperate to make an impact. It wasn't neccesarily a seller's market on run stopping DTs, but they made it one anyway.

#2811. Warriors hit with sudden injury blow as key rotation piece likely headed for surgery

Just as the Warriors were beginning to hit their stride, a surprise appeared on the injury report Sunday night that threatened to halt their momentum in its tracks.

Will Richard was suddenly listed as out for Golden State's game against the Pelicans on Monday night and will likely miss more time than that. The second-year guard was diagnosed with a torn ulnar collateral ligament in his left thumb, though the Warriors have not commented.

The injury isn't uncommon among NBA players and typically requires surgery that comes with a recovery timeline in the neighborhood of six weeks. However, Richard did seem to catch somewhat of a break, given that it occurred in his non-shooting hand.

Will Richard of the Golden State Warriors handles the ball against the Orlando Magic at Chase Center in San Francisco on Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

Richard, 23, had been playing well since a slow start to the season and carved out a role in coach Steve Kerr's rotation. He was in their starting lineup two of their past three games.

He attempted only one shot in 12 minutes in Saturday's win over the Magic but showed no sign of injury, making his sudden appearance on the injury report heading into Monday night a surprise.

Will Richard of the Golden State Warriors arrives at Chase Center before a game against the Miami Heat in San Francisco on Thursday, Nov. 5, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

The Warriors have won seven in a row since losing their first three games of the season, and Richard has been a small part of the turnaround. Although he has scored in double figures only twice, the former 56th overall pick has earned praise for his hustle and basketball IQ.

In 17.2 minutes per game, Richard was averaging 5.1 points. He appeared in 69 games as a rookie, starting 21, and averaged 6.4 points in 20 minutes per game, cooling down after a hot start.

Gui Santos, who recently returned from knee tendinitis, reclaimed his spot in the starting lineup Saturday against the Magic and will likely pick up some of Richard's minutes. The rest will be spread around between De'Anthony Melton, Brandon Williams, LJ Cryer and Gary Payton II.

Stephen Curry, left, and Will Richard of the Golden State Warriors high five during a game against the Miami Heat at Chase Center on Nov. 5, 2026, in San Francisco. NBAE via Getty Images

Kerr opted to insert Richard, a 6-foot-3 guard, into the starting lineup while Santos, a 6-foot-7 wing, was forced to miss two games with knee tendinitis. The silver lining for Golden State was that Richard's injury coincided with Santos' return in their win over the Magic.

The Warriors, however, should prepare to play without Richard for an extended period.

#2809. SC Freiburg vs. Union Berlin preview: Freiburg look to continue title push against struggling Union

Ironically, the 640km separating Berlin and Freiburg im Breisgau is a pretty accurate representation of where the teams currently sit on the table: with a win, Freiburg can push their way back into 3rd place as they continue hunting for a UEFA Champions League spot and maintain their pressure on Dortmund and Bayern, while Union Berlin could potentially sink back down into the relegation zone depending on Schalke’s outcome.

Will we see a shock at the Europa-Park Stadion, or will we see the points gap between both sides continue to widen?

Let’s begin with the home side, SC Freiburg.

Freiburg have been on a burner to commence the 2026/27 season with 5 wins, 3 draws and a solitary loss only coming against champions Bayern München, along with a respectable +8GD (18 goals scored). In addition to this, Freiburg ensured their Pokal survival by staving off recently relegated Wolfsburg with a last-gasp winner by Keisuke Gotō. The team from Baden-Württemberg also had a healthy start to their Conference League campaign with 2 wins and 1 loss, their most recent result being a 1-0 away win against Trabzonspor.

To go back to the Bundesliga, Freiburg managed to overcome a 1-0 deficit to Augsburg to come away from Bavaria with a 2-1 win in their most recent match. A win against Union Berlin would see them not only move back up to 3rd, but also come to within 2 points of league leaders Borussia Dortmund and Bayern München.

Apart from their 3-1 loss to Bayern München, Freiburg have been otherwise flawless at home and look poised to strike while the iron’s hot as they push for a first-ever UCL qualification.

A duo to watch this match will certainly be the strike partnership of Igor Matanović and Yuito Suzuki, who account for 8 goals (4 goals each) - nearly half of Freiburg’s total goals this season.

SC Freiburg will be without Florent Muslija and Patrick Osterhage.

Ironically, the club known as “Eisern Union” (Iron Union) seem to have let their iron cool down as they sit near the bottom of the table as one of the league's most out of form teams.

Since shocking the footballing world by qualifying for the UCL in the 2022/23 season, Union Berlin has in fact looked quite malleable in recent seasons (I promise that’s the last metal-related joke I will make!), with this season shaping up to be a relegation survival battle for the Berliners.

With only 1 win in 8 games thus far (4D, 3L), Union squandered their previous Bundesliga matchup against 1. FC Köln at home, losing out on some much needed points with direct relegation rivals. This followed a 5-0 battering by that very same Köln team in the Pokal less than a week prior.

The bad news for Union continues if we look at their away form thus far - in just 3 matches, Union has drawn 1 and lost 2, with a shocking -11 goal differential (1 scored, 12 conceded), by far the worst in the league.

With local rivals Hertha Berlin having just beaten top-of-the-table rivals Wolfsburg and continuing their flawless run in the 2. Bundesliga (10W, 1D, and a +16 GD), the pressure is surely beginning to mount on Union securing their status as Berlin’s best team.

Union Berlin will miss the likes of Andrej Iic, Stanley Nsoki, Andrik Markgraf and Robert Skov. Felix Uduokhai remains out via suspension after his red card against Augsburg.

To give Union fans a bit of hope, Freiburg have had a shaky record in recent years against the team from Berlin.

Union have been flawless against Freiburg in their last 7 matches (4 wins, 3 draws, and outscoring Freiburg 9:4), with their last loss occurring nearly 4 years ago to the day. Union is also currently on a 2-game winning streak against Freiburg when playing in the Europa-Park Stadion.

This does come with a caveat however, which is that both sides were a lot closer in regards to form and table positioning in the same fixture last season - in 2025/26, Freiburg was 8th (34 points, -5 GD) while Union was 11th (28 points, -12 GD). This time, 16th place Union (7 points, -12 GD) will really have to punch up if they want to win against 4th place Freiburg (16 points, +8 GD).

If Union Berlin are to regain their form, perhaps they can make it happen against Freiburg and potentially eke out another win to pick up some much needed points and create some separation at the bottom of the table.

#2807. NFL reinstates WR Brandon Aiyuk from reserve/left squad list

Brandon Aiyuk is back, and the ball is in the 49ers’ court.

The NFL reinstated the 49ers receiver from the reserve/left squad list on Monday. The league also gave the 49ers a two-week roster exemption, giving the team a window for making a decision about what to do with Aiyuk.

The 49ers now have an immediate decision to make about Aiyuk. If he’s on the roster on Tuesday at 4:00 p.m. ET, he’s owed a game check for the week.

Based on the Player Personnel Manual, placement on the reserve/left squad list apparently tolled his 2025 salary. That means he’ll return to the $1.17 million base salary from 2025, with the 2026 salary of $26.015 million delayed until 2027.

It gives the 49ers a window to trade Aiyuk, with the new team getting him for the rest of the season at only $585,000 in non-guaranteed pay.

The 49ers can carry Aiyuk as an extra man on the roster until Monday, November 23.

It’s unclear, currently, whether Aiyuk agreed to refund past bonus money to the 49ers in order to get the league to reinstate him. If the 49ers trade or release Aiyuk, the 49ers lose the ability to get money back from Aiyuk.

#2805. Week in Review: Spurs continue to return to form with another undefeated week

Welcome to Week in Review: a Monday feature that looks back at the week that was for the San Antonio Spurs, takes a look at the week ahead, and more. Enjoy!

Week 2: After a rough opening week of the season, the Spurs took advantage of an easy schedule against the sinking Kings and Jazz and injured Timberwolves to get right again, going undefeated with Victor Wembanyama absolutely dominating by averaging 36 points per game to win Western Conference Player of the Week.

It was a rare game where Wemby was not hot, with just 12 points 5-15 shooting, but the Spurs didn't need him to be in another match-up against the lowly Jazz. He deferred to the hot hands, and they used a balanced attack with all five starters plus Harrison Barnes and Keldon Johnson scoring in double figures, as well as dominant first and third quarters to put the game away early.

Unlike the week prior, the Mavericks had Cooper Flagg and Kyrie Irving available this time, and the match-up lived up to the hype. The Spurs dominated the first half and led by as much as 19, but their inability to protect big leads reared its ugly head again as it completely evaporate in the second half, not helped by the Spurs being ice-cold from three (5-36). But, Wemby once again proved he's on another tier this season with a historic 53-point, 24-rebound, 8-block night to rescue his team from the jaws of defeat (although it would have been easier had he made his last 4 free throws).

124-109 win at Sacramento Kings (NBA Emirates Cup)

In their first Tournament game of the the season, the Spurs made quick work of the cellar-dwelling Kings on the first night of a back-to-back to allow their players to rest in a game that wasn't as close as the final score indicates (although point-differential does matter in the Cup standings, and the Warriors now lead the group thanks to a 17-point win). Wemby again dominated, with 30 points and 14 rebounds in a quick 26 minutes of work.

After a sluggish first half featuring two teams on a back-to-back, poor shooting and a heavy whistle (for a total of 41 first-half free throws), the Spurs exploded in the second half against their first-round opponents from last season. Another dominant Wemby game and 17 threes helped the Spurs get out to a 30-point lead in the third quarter, leaving the Portland crowd in stunned silence and heading for the exists early.

John Schuhmann, NBA.com — 1 (last week: 4)

OffRtg: 117.2 (5) DefRtg: 107.1 (5) NetRtg: +10.1 (3)

The Spurs' winning streak is at eight games, over which they've ranked in the top three on both ends of the floor. They're 8-2 for the second straight season, though they have a better point differential than they had through 10 games last year.

1. Victor Wembanyama scored 52 points (adding 24 rebounds and eight blocks) against the Mavs on Wednesday … with a broken jump shot. After that game, he was 7-for-29 (24%) from mid-range and 12-for-50 (24%) from beyond the arc. But he scored 45 of those 52 points in the restricted area (13-for-16) or at the free throw line (19-for-23), and he may have found his shooting stroke over the weekend. He wasn't as dominant inside, but made seven of his 12 3-point attempts in Sacramento and Portland.2. The Spurs' starting lineup still doesn't play a ton of minutes; at 10.5 minutes per game, it ranks 13th among lineups that have played at least five games together. It was also outscored by the Kings on Friday. But it's allowed just 85.8 points per 100 possessions, the best mark among the 22 lineups that have played at least 50 total minutes, and it's been much better offensively (114.9 scored per 100) than the Spurs were when De'Aaron Fox, Dylan Harper and Stephon Castle shared the floor last season.

#2802. Eagles trade DT Jordan Davis to Jaguars

The Jaguars have gone all-in. And, as to 2026, the Eagles have checked out.

Via Adam Schefter of ESPN, the Eagles have traded defensive tackle Jordan Davis to the Jaguars. In return, the Eagles will acquire a first-round pick in 2027 and a second-round pick in 2029.

It’s the second trade the Jaguars have made today for a defensive starter. The day began with the Jaguars sending a 2027 second-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick to the Falcons for safety Jessie Bates III, continued with a deal to acquire defensive tackle Davon Gochaux from the Saints for a 2028 sixth-round pick, and culminated in the blockbuster deal for Davis.

Unlike Bates, who is in the last year of his contract, Davis signed a new deal in March, a three-year, $78 million extension. The Eagles paid an $18.79 million signing bonus and half of Davis’s $1.215 million base salary.

The move will trigger a $15.428 million cap charge for the Eagles in 2027.

While the trade complicates further trades of significant veteran contracts, the willingness to deal Davis underscores the fact that most players are available for the right price, and that few are untouchable.

As to Davis, the Eagles happily converted the player to a first-round pick in the next draft and a second-round pick two years after that.

For the Jaguars, Monday’s trade will do little to keep them off the Super Bowl radar screen. Even without Bates and Davis, the Jaguars were a legitimate contender to make it to the Super Bowl. They will now become one of the favorites to win it all.

The Eagles have lost seven in a row. Trading Davis won’t do much to spark a turnaround. Some believed they had embarked on a soft tank. The tank has now gotten a little more concrete.

#2801. The Lakers have the best minimum contract player in the NBA

In a salary-capped league, or "apron world" as president of basketball operations Rob Pelinka once referred to it, you need players to outperform their contracts. Even more so when they're on minimum deals.

Besides LeBron James, who is not your normal minimum signing, it can be argued the Lakers have the best one in the league in Matisse Thybulle.

The two-time All-Defensive selection has started every game for the 9-2 Lakers, who own the best record in the NBA. That includes multiple back-to-backs, despite his health being a primary concern when he signed.

His defense has been as advertised. LA had their most impressive win of the season Sunday. Without superstar Luka Dončić, they went in and beat the 6-2 Pistons on the road.

While only scoring four points, he collected four steals in his 28 minutes. It was his fourth game already this season with at least three takeaways.

The purple and gold force the second most turnovers in the league. Thybulle's relentless pursuit and, at times, unorthodox style is a major reason why. Watch his activity below on the first defensive possession of the game.

While closing out to the initial offensive player, he allows him to get a step in front of him to poke the ball away from behind. It's a defensive approach you would never teach, but is successful due to the combination of feel, a seven-foot wingspan and great hands.

Then, the lazy flat angle pass is deflected and eventually stolen. Thybulle leads the Lakers in steals and deflections and is tied for third in the entire league in the latter.

Like a hawk waiting in the wings for its prey, Thybulle will subtly maneuver himself into position to get a steal. He's taken numerous full court passes by opponents by making the read and pouncing after the pass is already in flight.

The best defenders in the league can somehow operate in two places at once. Watch below as he not only takes away the outlet to the corner, but then comes back to deflect and steal the dump off pass to the rim.

Two-man combinations are mainly thought of in terms of offensive fits, but Thybulle and Walker Kessler have been a defensive match made in heaven. The Lakers have an 11.8 net rating with both of them on the court, holding a 108.9 defensive rating, which would rank in the top 10 in the league.

With Kessler behind, Thybulle is free to poke at the ball and be his aggressive self. At times, it can be a detriment to the team with his gambles, but overall, it fits into a more active than reactive defensive identity.

LA throws him on the best opposing ball handlers every night at the point of attack. Watch him below on Cade Cunningham, who was held to just 14 points on 6-15 shooting Sunday.

Good defense is not always about getting the steal, but deterring the offensive player from getting to their spot on the floor. Thybulle forces Cunningham to change directions laterally twice and gets a hand on the pass to the corner. While the Pistons score on a putback, LA held the Pistons to just 36% shooting in the first half.

In addition to the steals and deflections, Thybulle is second on the team with nine blocks. He comes from the weak side at times to protect the rim and recovers ground to block 3-point attempts on the perimeter.

As the superb defense was expected, the offense has blown up his expected value. He's been a ball mover and connector, while finding his spots as a cutter against compromised defenses.

The shooting, while it's cooled off a bit, is still at 39.5% on catch-and-shoot 3-pointers. He's even added a no-dip 3-pointer, as shown below. It allows for a quicker release against potential close outs from defenders.

It's a long season, and after playing just 45 games total over the last two years, Thybulle still has to make it through the 82-game grind. So far, he's far outkicked his salary.

The defense will always be there, but if the shooting holds, the Lakers may have a championship-caliber wing on a minimum contract.

#2800. NFL Injury Report, Week 10: Latest news, updates for Puka Nacua, Patrick Mahomes, more players for fantasy football

Mahomes: At the time of Mahomes' injury in Week 9, it looked as though we were watching a QB who may be done for the season. #2798. What Coco Gauff was overheard saying to herself during win over Jessica Pegula which explains why she’s so good

Coco Gauff is seeking to win a second WTA Finals title in Indian Wells this week.

The American took the first step towards accomplishing her goal after beating Jessica Pegula on Sunday evening.

In what was a back and forth affair, Gauff eventually triumphed 6-4, 6-7, 6-2 in California.

Following Sunday's contest, Andrea Petkovic and Brad Gilbert were full of praise for the 22-year-old American star.

"I had a great seat next to Garbine Muguruza, tournament director here, and I saw one thing with Coco, and I was like, 'Man, that's why she's so good,' former world number nine Andrea Petkovic said on the Big T Podcast.

"So she came back to 5-5, [then] 6-5, and she was basically returning to break Jess's serve that she had just broken twice and back to back to win the match, so very important game, right?

"[At] 6-5 in the second set, Jess hits an okay, medium good wide first serve, and Coco misses a forehand return, and she turned around and I could read her lips, and she kind of clapped and said to herself, 'That was a good serve' about Jess's serve.

"And that was such an insight into her mind because every other player that I know, me including, would have reacted like, 'How can I miss this return?' And especially when you know they're going to serve it there."

"And you know what is special about Coco too is how resilient she was," Brad Gilbert, the former coach of Andre Agassi, Andy Murray and Coco Gauff, added. "What about that hold at 4-4 in the first set? 0-40 and then you knew she was going to break the next game.

"But I thought she did a great job of just battling in that second set, 5-1 down to even put herself in that position to close out the second set, and I do think fitness will play a factor in this tournament.

"It is nice that you know the ladies that a lot of them play six, seven, eight weeks in Asia, all outdoors on hardcore. So we're here on the same surface, but it was almost 90 degrees today.

2022 – lost to Iga Swiatek, Caroline Garcia and Daria Kasatkina in the group stage.

2023 – defeated Ons Jabeur and Marketa Vondrousova in the group stage before losing to Jessica Pegula in the semi-finals

2024 – won her first WTA Finals crown, beating Zheng Qinwen 3-6, 6-4, 7-6 in the final

2025 – exited in the group stage after losing to Aryna Sabalenka and Jessica Pegula

2026 – won her first match against Jessica Pegula, 6-4, 6-7, 6-2

"If you haven't played a tournament in three weeks, you can't simulate match play. So all of a sudden, it's like it can hit you, and I I definitely think Jess's legs were a little bit weary in the end of that."

Gauff will look to secure her place in the semi-finals when she competes against Marta Kostyuk and Linda Noskova later this week.

The two-time Grand Slam champion has a 2-1 record against Noskova and a 4-2 record against Kostyuk.

Gauff is set to play Kostyuk on Tuesday, following the conclusion of Pegula's match against Noskova.

Gauff was made to work hard by Pegula on Sunday, before the the 22-year-old demonstrated once again why she is one of the most dogged players on the WTA Tour.

As per OptaAce, Gauff has now tied Pegula for the most three-set victories this year. Both Gauff and Pegula have won 19 three-set matches throughout 2026.

Gauff and Pegula could play each other again before the end of 2026. However, they would both need to reach the final of the WTA Finals to do so.

Realistically, after losing on Sunday, Pegula will need to defeat Marta Kostyuk on Tuesday to stand any chance of advancing to the semi-finals in California.

#2797. The NBA’s anti-tanking rules might thwart the Sixers’ buyout dreams

Set aside their ugly loss to the Cleveland Cavaliers in the NBA Cup on Friday, and the Sixers are otherwise having a largely dreamy start to the season. They still sit atop the Eastern Conference at 9-2, and they've shown a far higher ceiling on defense than anyone expected this early in the season.

The Sixers have already hit a critical benchmark, too. This is the fifth time in LeBron James' career that he's been on a team that started the season 7-1, according to ESPN's Kris Rhim. All four of the previous teams that he's done it with made the NBA Finals that season.

As long as their starting five stays healthy, the Sixers figure to be a preferred landing spot for ring-chasers later this year. They already have one open roster spot, and they could create another one by salary-dumping Justin Edwards ahead of the trade deadline. If Kevin Love is already queued up as one future signing, the Sixers might prefer to have additional flexibility to explore all of their pre- and post-deadline options.

However, the NBA's new draft-lottery system could impact which players are available on the buyout market.

"Philadelphia would be an attractive destination in the buyout market after the Feb. 11 trade deadline, when the Sixers could squeeze a pro-rated minimum onto their books," ESPN's Tim MacMahon wrote Friday. "But several executives around the league wondered how much the new anti-tanking lottery rules will weaken the buyout market."

The new lottery punishes the teams with the three worst records by giving them lower odds than the other seven teams that miss the play-in tournament. They can fall no lower than No. 12—the other 13 teams can drop as far as No. 16—but they have only a 16 percent chance at a top-three pick, a 28 percent chance at a top-five pick and a 61 percent chance at a top-10 pick. The other non-play-in teams have a 24 percent chance at a top-three pick, a 39 percent chance at a top-five pick and a 73 percent chance at a top-10 pick.

Teams that don't control their first-round pick in 2027—a group that includes the Brooklyn Nets, Los Angeles Clippers, Milwaukee Bucks and Phoenix Suns—shouldn't be particularly concerned with the new lottery rules. The second round will now be arranged in reverse order, meaning whichever team lands the No. 1 pick will drop to No. 46 in the second round, but that shouldn't deter teams without a 2027 first-round pick from selling off veterans at the trade deadline.

The question is how many teams will buy out their unwanted veterans on expiring contracts if they're unable to offload them at the trade deadline.

Sacramento Kings guard Zach LaVine might be the big test of the new system this year. The Kings are already flying dead stick, and it might be tough for them to find a taker for LaVine's $49.0 million expiring contract by the trade deadline. However, they have control over their own 2027 first-round pick, which means they have clear incentive not to finish with a bottom-three record. Would they keep LaVine around after the trade deadline to help them accomplish that?

Granted, even if the buyout market is largely limited to teams that don't control their own first-round pick in 2027, the Sixers could still have some decent options available to them. Clippers center Brook Lopez is on the tail end of his career and wouldn't do much to help the Sixers' rebounding issues, but he can space the floor and protect the rim, which would give them a valuable change-up whenever Joel Embiid is off the floor.

The Clippers presumably wouldn't want to waive Max Strus, Derrick Jones Jr. or Kris Dunn, if only to ensure they maintain their Bird rights on them heading into free agency next summer. The same goes for the Nets with Michael Porter Jr. and the Chicago Bulls with Jalen Smith. But agents will still have the same motivation to get their clients to winning environments, even if teams have less incentive to buy out their veterans on expiring deals.

Whenever the NBA implements a rule change as drastic as the new draft lottery, it inevitably has trickle-down effects, many of which are unintended consequences. It's unclear if the league intended to whittle down the buyout market as part of its anti-tanking push, but executives are already wondering how it will look this year, as MacMahon relayed Friday.

The Sixers can only hope those fears are unfounded. Otherwise, they might have to pivot to a plan B regarding in-season improvements to their roster.

Unless otherwise noted, all stats via NBA.com, PBPStats, Cleaning the Glass or Basketball Reference. All salary information via Spotrac and salary-cap information via RealGM.

#2796. Eagles say Jaguars made an offer they couldn't refuse for Jordan Davis

The Eagles announced the trade that sent defensive tackle Jordan Davis to the Jaguars, saying the “Jaguars made an offer that executive vice president/General Manager Howie Roseman simply could not refuse.”

The Jaguars are sending a 2027 first-round pick and a 2029 second-round pick to the Eagles.

“We are extremely grateful to Jordan for his contributions to our team on the field and in the Philadelphia community since we drafted him five years ago,” the team said in a statement. “He has been a key contributor to our team culture and on-field success, which included two Super Bowl appearances and a world championship in 2024. His growth and development as both a player and a leader are a testament to his hard work and character. We wish Jordan nothing but continued success.”

The Eagles now have first-round picks in the 2027 draft, two first-round picks and two second-round picks in the 2028 draft and multiple second-round picks in 2029.

The Eagles made Davis a first-round pick in 2022, and he totaled 193 tackles, 9.5 sacks, 15 quarterback hits and 11 passes defensed in 73 games.

#2795. Washington Commanders Whiteboard: Notes and observations from Week 9

Before the advent of media devices, football coaches at all levels used simple whiteboards to outline plays, map out player positions and explain schemes in real time. Nowadays digital playbooks and tablets have taken over, but coaches still use whiteboards on occasion to impart information quickly and effectively. The following are some quick notes and observations from the Washington Commanders' 13-2 loss to the LA Rams at home.

Make no mistake, Puka Nucua is one of the best wide receivers in the league, and he is going to get his yards. This was a known fact heading into the matchup, but Daronte Jones and Washington's defense knew the key was to simply limit him. Jones spoke openly about it in his Thursday presser, but when it came time to contain the All-Pro wide receiver, the defense appeared to have no idea how to accomplish it.

Puka torched Washington's secondary, hauling in 14 receptions for 150 receiving yards and one touchdown, and the Rams wide receiver didn't have to work particularly hard to do it. The Commanders played zone coverage throughout the game and rarely tested Nacua with man or double coverage, and while Washington was successful limiting his yards after the catch, he picked them apart underneath.

How did the rest of the Rams receivers fare? They combined for 78 total receiving yards and no touchdowns. There was simply no need to target them, as Washington's defense never found a way to force the issue. The Rams ran the ball effectively in the second half with Kyren Williams, Blake Corum, and Nucua combining for 123 yards on the ground. It was enlightening to watch Sean McVay run a clinic on how to properly execute an offense with a heavy personnel emphasis.

While some analysts were quick to point out that the defense wasn't the reason the Commanders lost the game on Sunday, their performance is indicative of how coordinator Daronte Jones is unable to adjust his game plan from week to week. The pressure didn't get home, the secondary couldn't stop their playmakers, and the defense was reliant on turnovers to compete. Conversely, Minnesota DC Brain Flores changes his game plans weekly, and at times will shift away from his preferred zone schemes in favor of more man coverage when the matchup calls for it.

When David Blough was promoted to offensive coordinator during the offseason, there was a lot of discussion about the number of quality offensive coordinators he had played or worked under and which he would draw from to build his own offensive scheme, including Jeff Brohm, Kevin O'Connell, Ben Johnson and even Kliff Kingsbury. Nine weeks into the season, fans are still wondering what Blough is trying to accomplish on the offensive side of the ball.

At times, the Commanders' offense leans into heavy personnel and under center looks before reverting to shotgun-based schemes with 11 personnel when things don't go according to plan. Some plays are well-called and timely; others make little sense for the situation or the defensive looks. Blough spoke openly of wanting to utilize the screen game more, but never went back to it after having success early, and only one team has a lower percentage of screens than Washington this season.

The Commanders are also supposedly implementing a wide-zone rushing attack, but with little to no evidence of it on the field or that offensive line coach Darnell Stapleton knows how to implement it. They actually found some success early in this matchup, then inexplicably went away from their running game later in the game. The pass protection also struggled and while a good portion of the problem is certainly on the offensive personnel, part of being an effective coordinator is scheming plays to maximize your strengths while providing a cohesive plan for the offense every week. The game plan versus the Rams should have called for chip blocks to help Brandon Coleman on the edge and designed roll-outs to move Daniels out of the pocket. Instead, fans saw very little of either one.

So far, David Blough seems to be simply calling plays instead of coordinating an NFL offense. The Week 5 edition of Whiteboard called for Anthony Lynn or another consultant to assist the rookie coordinator. Headed into Week 10, it may now be too late.

Similarly, the Week 1 Whiteboard article called out the fact that, while many of the faces had changed at the coordinator level, the same problems from last season persisted. Those include pre-snap penalties, and while Week 9 wasn't quite as bad as the opener, the Commanders still had their fair share of false starts on their own home field. Meanwhile, other issues were also persistent and equally damaging. Both Stefon Diggs and Antonio Williams dropped catchable balls on critical downs, while looking ineffective at run blocking. Diggs also questionably tried to run for more yards instead of stepping out of bounds in the final moments of the game. Fans shouldn't be surprised if Diggs is not invited back for another season, which would potentially have the wide receiver looking for his fourth new team in as many years.

It wasn't all on Diggs. Dan Quinn also burned what would have been a valuable timeout earlier in the game by challenging a play that was clearly not going to be overturned. Once again, the Commanders were short on timeouts during the critical final moments of the game.

The issues at the center spot had faded after the addition of Lucas Patrick in Week 2, but came back front and center after Patrick delivered a number of high snaps before leaving the game with a possible concussion. Instead of Nick Allegretti, who had received the unwavering support the coaching staff and front office all offseason, Patrick was replaced with sixth-round rookie Matt Gulbin. This effectively ended the ability of Washington's offense to move the ball in the fourth quarter, as Rams' defensive coordinator Chris Shula struck quickly and effectively.

Whiteboard Week 2 concluded with this same question, following a game in which Jayden Daniels dislocated his elbow for a second time. At that point, it was theorized the team could make a comeback if Marcus Mariota could find a way to win a game or two and Daniels received positive news on a possible return. Both happened, but Dan Quinn and the rest of the Washington Commanders have failed to capitalize on the several opportunities to win games since then, including the Giants in Week 5, the Niners in Week 6 and now the LA Rams in Week 9. Despite all of that, the Commanders still have a chance to win their division, starting with a critical matchup this Thursday versus the Giants.

New York and Washington both have the same remaining strength of schedule, so a win on Thursday night and a loss by the Dallas Cowboys to the 49ers in Week 10 could help the Commanders gain ground in the division race. Washington would also get a full 10 days to prepare for what would become a critical matchup versus the Cincinnati Bengals on Monday Night Football.

A loss would leave Washington with no hope of reaching the playoffs this season and the burden of correcting the current issues with the team would fall to members of leadership higher up in the organization.

#2792. Miami reportedly to host NBA All-Star Game in 2028 for first time since 1990

The NBA All-Star Game is returning to Miami.

Per ESPN's Shams Charania, the NBA is finalizing Miami as the host city of the league's 2028 All-Star Weekend.

Despite being a prime destination for a February party, this will mark just the second time that Miami has hosted the NBA's All-Star weekend. The first and only other time was in 1990, the second season of the Heat's existence.

Magic Johnson won MVP in that game, while David Robinson, Reggie Miller, Dennis Rodman and Scottie Pippen all made their All-Star debuts.

Miami will host the NBA All-Star Game in 2028 for the second time.

Since Miami last hosted, 10 NBA cities have hosted multiple All-Star weekends: Los Angeles (4 times), New Orleans (3 times), Atlanta, Charlotte, Cleveland, Houston, New York, Orlando, Phoenix and Salt Lake City (2 times each). The Bay Area has also hosted twice since 1990 with games in Oakland and in San Francisco.

Phoenix will host again in February from the 19-21.

As currently iterated, the NBA All-Star weekend officially consists of the Rising Stars Game on Friday night, the skills contest, 3-point contest and dunk contest on All-Star Saturday and the All-Star Game itself on Sunday.

#2791. Caleb Downs makes his mark in crucial victory over Colts

After a demoralizing loss to the lowly Arizona Cardinals a week prior, the Dallas Cowboys needed a bounce-back performance in the worst way on Sunday. Sitting at 3-5, Dallas had to leave Indianapolis with a win if they wanted any hope of staying in the playoff hunt.

While it was not always pretty, the Cowboys showed up on both sides of the ball when it mattered most. Both the offense and defense came through late in the fourth quarter, leading to a crucial 27-23 win over the Colts.

The Cowboys had some standout players on both sides of the ball, but rookie safety Caleb Downs had his first signature game. Downs, playing against his older brother Josh for the first time in their professional careers, was a leading force in the victory, earning him this week's game ball award.

Expectations were sky-high for Downs at the beginning of his rookie campaign. While the 21-year-old had shown flashes of greatness in Dallas' first eight games, we'd yet to see him take over a game as he did at Ohio State. For the first time in his NFL career, Downs did just that on Sunday.

Downs and Quinnen Williams were the best defenders on the field on Sunday afternoon. Downs showed his ability to impact the game in multiple ways, finishing with a sack, a PBU, and a game-sealing interception in the fourth quarter.

Downs finished the game with the highest coverage grade of any defender, recording a remarkable 90.6 mark. We've seen the Ohio State product struggle a bit in coverage early on in his career, but Sunday was a different story. Indianapolis completed just one of four passes when targeting Downs for a total of 12 yards.

If the Cowboys defense wants to get closer to league-average moving forward, Caleb Downs playing like he did on Sunday will be a huge help.

#2789. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2782. Army-Navy game moves up a week to accommodate expanded CFP

One second-order effect of the College Football Playoff expanding to 24 teams is the annual Army-Navy game, which has traditionally been held on the second weekend of December, following conference championship weekend. With ESPN and the CFP officially announcing the expansion to 24 teams on Monday, the Army-Navy game also officially got its new date.

Next year's Army-Navy game will be played on Saturday, Dec. 4 at Lincoln Financial Field in Philadelphia, one week earlier than the originally scheduled date of Dec. 11. The game, which is broadcast annually by CBS, will now be played the week following rivalry week, which is the last official week of the regular season. Army-Navy will now occupy the Saturday that used to be reserved for a full slate of conference championship games.

Where to put the Army-Navy game in an expanded CFP format was often a footnote in discussions about potential expansion until President Trump signed an executive order earlier this year mandating that the annual matchup between the two service academies have its own standalone time slot, free of any other FBS games. Moving the game to the first weekend of December should allow for that, as conference championship games will no longer occupy those television windows.

"We are thankful to the city of Philadelphia and Lincoln Financial Field for their efforts to manage the date change of the 2027 Army-Navy Game to ensure the Game is played in its exclusive time slot on CBS," Tom Theodorakis, director of athletics and chief executive officer of Army West Point Athletic Association, said in an announcement. "As we look ahead to future games, we are excited about the opportunity to build on our strong foundation and secure new commitments that demonstrate a shared passion to grow the game and drive economic impact in host cities."

"We appreciate the flexibility of the people of Philadelphia to accommodate this date change, and we applaud the collaboration between the city of Philadelphia, Lincoln Financial Field and the academies," Michael Kelly, Director of Athletics of the Naval Academy and President of the Naval Academy Athletic Association, said in Monday's release. "We are also extremely grateful to the CFP leadership for their collaboration. Finding a way to preserve the exclusive broadcast window for America's Game while ensuring our program has a rightful path to compete in the College Football Playoff is a tremendous outcome for our student-athletes and fans across the country."

The post Army-Navy game moves up a week to accommodate expanded CFP appeared first on Awful Announcing.

#2780. Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls

Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Still no losing streaks to speak of for the 2026-27 Sixers.

The Sixers earned a 127-121 win Sunday over the Bulls to wrap up a 2-1 road trip. They moved to 9-2 overall and Chicago fell to 1-8.

Every Sixers starter scored in double figures.

Three reached the 20-point mark in Jaylen Brown (29 points), Tyrese Maxey (27 points) and LeBron James (22). Joel Embiid had 18 points and 10 rebounds.

Josh Giddey posted 19 points, 15 assists and seven rebounds for the Bulls.

The Sixers were without Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Bulls' absences included Norman Powell (right shoulder inflammation/soreness) and Zach Collins (respiratory illness).

Next on the Sixers' schedule is a home game Tuesday night vs. the Celtics. Here are observations on their win over Chicago:

Following his 0-for-9 night Friday in the Sixers' loss to the Cavaliers, Embiid did not snap right back into form.

He was well short on his first shot attempt, a wide-open three-pointer. Embiid then failed to score in the post over Jalen Smith.

Embiid got a field goal to go at the 6:42 mark of the first quarter when he tipped in his own miss. However, Smith canned a three on the ensuing possession to give Chicago a 23-16 lead.

James committed two early turnovers, although he did sink a corner three-pointer during a fast-paced first quarter. VJ Edgecombe added two long-range jumpers in the first.

Entering Sunday, the Sixers' minus-9.6 net rating in the first quarter ranked 27th in the NBA. They managed to hold a one-point lead in Chicago after the first.

The Sixers' offensive rebounding was their greatest strength in the first quarter. They poured in 17 second-chance points. Embiid had four offensive boards in the opening frame.

Jabari Walker assumed backup center duties for the second straight game without Bona. He picked up where Embiid left off on the glass, grabbing seven rebounds (three offensive) in a great first stint.

Though he's undersized in the height department for an NBA center at 6-foot-7, Walker has plenty of rebounding tools.

He's listed at a strong 237 pounds, doesn't mind contact whatsoever, hunts the ball, and has a knack for winning contested boards. Walker showed all of that Sunday. Very hard to overstate how well he rebounded.

The Sixers' first-shot offense also had many bright moments in the second quarter.

James got rolling on his way to a 16-point first half, Embiid made a couple of mid-range jumpers, and Maxey hit two free throws put the Sixers up 60-50 late in the second period.

Chicago evened the game at 68-all early in the third quarter on a Matas Buzelis three, but the Bulls couldn't sustain that run.

The Bulls' defense continued to look like one of the worst in the NBA and the Sixers' well-balanced offense kept the scoreboard moving. Brown nailed a turnaround jumper to give his team a double-digit lead. Walker knocked down a top-of-the-key triple to extend the Sixers' advantage to 12 points.

The Sixers had two apparent injury scares in the second half. Brown limped to the sideline with 6:55 to go in the third quarter, but he stayed in the game. Edgecombe headed to the locker room after subbing out at the 9:08 mark of the fourth quarter. He returned to the Sixers' bench and ultimately returned for a final stint.

On the floor, the Sixers couldn't ease to victory. Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson trimmed Chicago's deficit to 116-113 by draining a three. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse called timeout.

The Sixers were efficient enough in the half court down the stretch.

Embiid made a put-back hoop and a pair of free throws. Maxey drilled a side-step three-ball after a wraparound feed from James. The Sixers converted their clutch free throws and secured a winning road trip.

#2779. Sean McVay on Puka Nacua injury: I'd be lying if I said I wasn't questioning that play call

Rams wide receiver Puka Nacua is set to go on injured reserve after suffering MCL, PCL and turf toe injuries in Sunday’s win over the Commanders.

Nacua’s injuries occurred on the Rams’ final offensive play when he was tackled after rushing for five yards on third-and-11. Nacua took a handoff on the play and head coach Sean McVay was asked at a Monday press conference if he second-guessed himself for putting Nacua in position to take a hit at that point in the game.

McVay said he’s “kicking myself about those kinds of things” and called it something the Rams have to think about “internally” as they move forward.

“I’d be lying to you if I said I haven’t questioned the decision-making on that last play over and over in my head of what could have been another option that you felt like could have gotten a conversion, and maybe ended the game that would’ve led to him not taking a violent hit to his right knee,” McVay said. “It’s stuff that I feel tremendous responsibility for, and we’ve got to be able to figure out how to protect these guys while also putting them in positions that help our team and allow them to be able to shine. He’s a guy we have to be intentional about because it’s a bummer. Because he can do so much, you want to make sure you don’t take that for granted.”

With Nacua and Davante Adams now out for the indefinite future, the Rams may be in the market for receiving help before the trade deadline passes at 4 p.m. ET on Tuesday. They do not have their 2027 first- or third-round picks, their 2028 second-round pick or their 2029 third-round pick thanks to their trades for Myles Garrett and Trent McDuffie.

#2776. Streaking Rams suddenly face a rough future after Nacua joins Adams on sideline with injuries

LOS ANGELES (AP) — The Los Angeles Rams' offense has relied on just two wide receivers more heavily than just about any good NFL team over the past two seasons.

Both of those wide receivers are now out indefinitely with injuries, and the Rams' Super Bowl dreams have also taken a devastating blow.

All-Pro Puka Nacua will be sidelined for at least a month after he sprained ligaments in his right knee and big toe on the final offensive snap of the Rams' 20-13 victory over the Washington Commanders on Sunday. He is likely headed to injured reserve to join Davante Adams, who has no set return date after incurring a noncontact hamstring injury last month.

Nacua's injury occurred right when the Rams (5-4) were finishing off their third consecutive victory and getting back on track toward their preseason goals with Myles Garrett's return from a seven-game absence.

Losing two playmaking receivers would be devastating to any team, but it's a particular problem for the Rams because they don't have an established No. 3 receiver — or really any receiver with whom Matthew Stafford has a strong on-field connection, at least judging by results.

Last season, Nacua and Adams made 78.4% of the catches by all of the wide receivers on Los Angeles' roster. No other wideout had more than 18 receptions.

This season, the disparity is even more stark: Nacua and Adams have made 100 of the Rams' 120 receptions (83.3%) by wideouts, and nobody else has more than Konata Mumpfield's 11 grabs.

Coach Sean McVay and Stafford throw aggressively and creatively to their tight ends and running backs, but losing the two biggest playmakers in the passing game will fundamentally change the way defenses can attack Los Angeles.

And even if the Rams throw off their reluctance to make a trade for a wideout this week, any new arrival would face a significant learning curve to become a contributor.

McVay realizes the challenge he faces over the next few weeks — but injury adversity perks up a competitive coach who relishes the chance to find clever new ways to move the ball. Roughly a year ago, he introduced three-tight-end sets that succeeded and were quickly copied around the league.

"One of the things that I have been proud of with our team is we've lost a lot of guys, and it's just about, 'Hey, let's figure out how to be in the moment and take it one day at a time,'" McVay said. "Adjust and adapt. Figure out how to put all the pieces together and get the outcome we're hunting up, whatever that looks like. And it might be different than maybe what people thought, or what our plans were."

The best wide receiver left on the Rams' roster is probably Tutu Atwell, but McVay has been notoriously reluctant to use the undersized pass-catcher during his six seasons with the team. Atwell got significant playing time at Washington and made two catches while having another reception called back by penalty, but he also dropped a key third down pass over the middle.

The other realistic options are Mumpfield and Jordan Whittington, who haven't produced consistently in their careers — and who don't have Atwell's elite speed.

The Rams' pass defense is getting the job done, currently ranking fifth in the league. Even without their top two cornerbacks because of injury, LA's secondary and pass rush repeatedly came up with big plays at Washington. Garrett's three sacks, multiple quarterback pressures and a forced fumble were the obvious game-changers, but Emmanuel Forbes and Kam Curl excelled downfield against their former team.

The Rams' surprising penalty problems continued with eight flags for 70 yards. The NFL's least-penalized team last season is currently the seventh worst this season with 71 penalties and fifth worst with 599 yards.

After a season of misadventure, the special teams units actually performed competently, from the two active kickers to the coverage units to Kyren Williams, who took over on punt returns and made four fair catches. The only major mistake was a holding penalty on Grant Stuard that wiped out Ronnie Rivers' 38-yard kickoff return. By Rams standards, that's outstanding.

The Rams' offense started slowly yet again, introducing pressure into another game this team should have dominated on paper. Los Angeles has scored one first-quarter touchdown since September. One week after a horrific first half against the Chargers, the Rams managed just two field goals in the first half and didn't get a lead or a third down conversion until the third quarter.

Left guard Steve Avila is also out indefinitely with a hamstring injury, hurting the Rams' running game and pass protection. Beaux Limmer is the likely replacement.

58.3% — Nacua's share of all the receptions made by Rams wide receivers this season. He has made 30% of the Rams' total receptions (70 of 232).

The Rams need to avenge their most embarrassing loss of the season when they travel to Arizona on Sunday — because after that, their weird schedule gets much tougher. They don't get a true bye week, instead returning to host Green Bay on Thanksgiving Eve, followed by a visit from Kansas City on the following Thursday.

#2775. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2773. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2770. Rams expect All-Pro WR Puka Nacua to land on injured reserve with multiple knee sprains and turf toe

The Los Angeles Rams expect to place All-Pro receiver Puka Nacua on injured reserve with MCL and PCL sprains to his right knee and turf toe.

Head coach Sean McVay made the announcement to reporters on Monday. Nacua suffered the injuries during Sunday's 20-13 win over the Washington Commanders. It's not clear how long Nacua will be sidelined, but a stint on injured reserve means he'll miss a minimum of four games.

The news of Nacua's expected IR placement is another significant blow to an offense that's already playing without starting receiver and former Pro Bowler Davante Adams. Now the Rams are facing an extended period without their top two weapons in the passing game.

A two-time All-Pro who led the NFL in receptions (129) and yards per game (107.2) in 2025, Nacua was in the midst of another standout campaign despite having missed two games early in the season.

In seven games this season, Nacua has 70 catches for 715 yards and 4 touchdowns. He had his most productive game of the season against Washington prior to his late-game injury with 14 catches for 150 yards and a touchdown. He also posted two carries for 20 yards.

Nacua appeared to sustain his injury on one of those carries on the Rams' final offensive play of the game. He took contact to his knee on a tackle and was able to quickly get up. ESPN's Adam Schefter reported after the game that Nacua sustained an injury and would undergo an MRI on Monday.

Adams, meanwhile is sidelined with a ruptured hamstring he sustained in a Week 6 loss to the Arizona Cardinals.

McVay announced on Oct. 19 that Adams was expected to miss six-to-10 weeks with his injury. He's missed three games since. The earliest Adams would return under that timeline would be a Week 12 game against the Green Bay Packers on Dec. 3, while a 10-week absence would keep him out through Week 17 against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers.

The earliest Nacua would be permitted to return from injured reserve would be a Week 15 game against the Dallas Cowboys on Dec. 20.

With both of them sidelined, Konata Mumpfield, Tutu Atwell and Jordan Whittington are the next men up on the depth chart. Mumpfield is the leading receiver among the three with 11 catches for 162 yards and a touchdown.

The Rams, meanwhile, are fighting to stay afloat in the NFC playoff picture after entering the season as the favorite to win the Super Bowl. Sunday's win over the Commanders was their third straight and improved their record to 5-4.

That leaves them on the outside looking in at the playoff picture behind the NFC West-leading San Francisco 49ers (7-1) and the 6-3 Packers, who would be the last NFC wild-card team if the playoffs started today.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2769. Lamb claims ‘Refs had it out for me’ in Week 9 Cowboys win

The game giveth, and the game taketh away. CeeDee Lamb was on the other end of both transactions in the Cowboys' wild win in Indianapolis.

The five-time Pro Bowler reeled in 10 catches on Sunday, posting 195 yards (his second-best day as a pro) and a touchdown in the 27-23 victory. With the Week 9 Monday night game still to play, Lamb leads the league in targets, is ranked third in receptions, second in yards, and is tied for second in touchdowns.

Still, he wasn't happy with what he said was his "most complicated" game as a pro, calling it an "up and down" performance.

There was the would-be touchdown catch in the third quarter where Lamb was unable to get his second foot down in the end zone before going out of bounds.

There were two rare offensive pass interference calls against him.

And, of course, there was the play everyone was talking about afterward, the 23-yard grab where Lamb dove to make the catch then realized he hadn't been touched. He got up and raced another 25 yards- seemingly on his way to the end zone- before being tripped up. He tried to brace himself as he fell, and he lost control of the football. The play initially ended as a 48-yard pickup, but after review, went in the books as a Lamb fumble recovered by the Colts.

"Before the review, I obviously thought I was down, because I felt- I don't know who it was; I think it was a linebacker- hit me in my back as I was trying to make a move, and I went down, right?" Lamb explained to reporters after the game. "But I used my arm, with the ball, which, obviously, I feel like I shouldn't have. But when you're in a moment of moving around, I guess, you're not really thinking. Obviously I know not to use the ball to keep my balance, but I also thought I was down by contact."

Replays clearly showed that it was the back of Lamb's wrist that touched the ground, leading to some confusion over whether that counts as part of the forearm, which would have ruled Lamb down at that spot.

"Great job finding the ball. Great job recognizing he wasn't down," Cowboys head coach Brian Schottenheimer said in his postgame press conference. "But the rule is the rule. Anything wrist and above: if it's on the ground, it's not a fumble. It's got to be your forearm. We were pretty clear that is was going to be a turnover."

Clear or not, Lamb was visibly upset over the gaffe, both on the sideline in the moments after the fumble and even in the tunnel leading to the locker room after the team had secured the win.

"That's who CeeDee is as a competitor," Schottenheimer told the media. "He said, 'Hey, I owe you one.'"

But Lamb had other moments to be mad about, too. Like those two pass interference calls. He's only ever been flagged for that particular infraction four times previously in his entire career: exactly once per season from 2021 to 2024.

On Sunday, he got hit for it twice by Clay Martin's crew.

"Clearly, the refs had it out for me this week, Lamb said.

"I don't think they were messing with me today, to be honest. Because there's been plenty of instances where I let the DB touch me first and then I create separation. So I'm just kind of confused: if I don't create separation [and] I just let him hold me, are they going to call a hold? I'm not even going to live in that realm of letting them hold me, because you can't, physically. You can't hold me."

Lamb started to expound on the subject further before realizing that he was treading into dangerous waters, possibly risking a heavy fine from the league (or giving away too much of his own technique) with what he was about to say next.

"I'm going to just make him-- No, I'm not going to give them that clip. Never mind. Never mind."

Between the failed toe-drag, the fumble, and the two OPIs, Lamb arguably left up to an additional three receptions, 51 extra yards, and two more touchdowns on the field at Lucas Oil Stadium.

It was already a monster day for the 27-year-old. It was almost the kind of game that is talked about for ages.

"Obviously, the numbers look crazy," Lamb acknowledged after the win. "But it could've been more. I'm always thinking about what could have been."

That's becoming a theme for the Cowboys' entire 2026 season.

Todd is on X at @ToddBrock24f7. Also, follow Cowboys Wire on Facebook to join in on the conversation with fellow fans!
