#2767. What waiving Tanner Arkin means for the Patriots

The New England Patriots made a change to their 53-man roster on Monday. Tight end Tanner Arkin, who had been with the team since his arrival as a rookie free agent earlier this year and had made the roster out of training camp, was waived.

Let's assess what the move means for the Patriots.

The Patriots had four tight ends on their active roster coming out of Week 9, and all of them had seen the field during the 45-27 loss to the Green Bay Packers on Sunday. However, only three of the group — Hunter Henry, Eli Raridon and Arkin — actually were employed on offense; the fourth tight end, Cameron Latu, played exclusively on special teams.

With Arkin now waived, the natural follow-up move would be to bump Latu up the depth chart and into a more expansive role as a third tight end and blocking option — one played by Arkin lately as well: in his five games so far, he played 50 snaps either run or pass blocking, and just 10 running routes.

That said, the Patriots might not turn to Latu to work as their TE3; the fact Arkin played over him can already be interpreted as the team not fully trusting him in such a role. Instead, New England might just keep its current arrangement in place and use first-round rookie offensive tackle Caleb Lomu as a sixth lineman and de facto blocking tight end (albeit without the occasional receiver rep).

Looking solely at the traditional tight ends on the Patriots' pay roll right now, we can see that the team is down to three on the active roster plus one each on the practice squad and on injured reserve. The hierarchy is pretty established among them, with Hunter Henry and Eli Raridon the clear 1-2 punch at the position.

53-man roster (3): Hunter Henry (85), Eli Raridon (82), Cameron Latu (36)

Should he pass through the waiver wire unclaimed, Arkin re-signing with New England's practice squad appears to be a foregone conclusion. In that case, the team would be allowed to standard-elevate him to the game day roster on three separate occasions during the regular season.

NFL teams can return up to eight players from injured reserve during the regular season, and the Patriots have taken advantage of that system four times so far. One would imagine that Arkin's freed-up roster spot — that puts New England at 52 players — goes to a player set to come back from IR.

The prime candidate at the moment appears to be interior offensive lineman Greg Van Roten, who returned to practice last week to open his 21-day activation window. With left guard Ben Brown not expected to be ready for New England's upcoming game in Germany against the Detroit Lions, more quality depth up front will be important and Van Roten would be able to provide just that.

Of course, the Patriots could also opt to go in a different direction. Arkin, after all, split his time between tight end, fullback and special teams this year. Maybe his spot goes to Reggie Gilliam, who is eligible to return from injured reserve as well and would be more of a 1-for-1 replacement.

When the Patriots trimmed their roster to 53 players in late August, three undrafted rookie free agents made the cut. Arkin was one of them, joining linebacker Khalil Jacobs and cornerback Channing Canada. Two months later, only Canada remains on the active team: Arkin was just let go, while Jacobs has been on injured reserve since before the start of the regular season.

At the moment, though, another rookie free agent is on the 53-man roster as well. Linebacker Erick Hunter was picked up by New England after his release from the Detroit Lions in early September, and has seen regular snaps on special teams since then.

#2766. Devils confound fans with bizarre explanation for Connor Hellebuyck sitting out of Winnipeg return

NEW YORK — The New Jersey Devils confounded fans with their explanation for why star goalie Connor Hellebuyck won't play Tuesday in his tense return to Winnipeg, claiming it "would potentially give us a reduction in our expected win probability."

The bizarre statement came from general manager Sunny Mehta, who said Sunday night that the team debated the "organizational decision" at length.

"Connor Hellebuyck was adamant that he felt a duty to play on Tuesday in Winnipeg," said Mehta, who previously served as the Devils' director of hockey analytics.

"However, in our estimation, playing in this game would potentially give us a reduction in our expected win probability."

The Devils acquired Hellebuyck — the 2024-25 NHL MVP and a four-time All-Star — from the Winnipeg Jets on Oct. 23 after he demanded a trade.

The Jets had suspended Hellebuyck after he didn't report to training camp, with the contentious saga drawing the ire of Winnipeg fans.

Tuesday's game in Winnipeg was thus expected to be fraught with acrimony, yet Hellebuyck made it clear that he wanted to play.

"But when you're looking at what's best for the team, I understand where they're coming from," Hellebuyck said Sunday. "I love Winnipeg. Playing there again, just facing the music, seeing the reaction, it'd be nice. But it's out of my hands."

Perhaps even more than their decision to sit Hellebuyck, the Devils' wordy and overly analytical statement didn't sit well with NHL fans — at all.

One social media user wrote that Mehta's reference to expected win probability "nearly ruined hockey for me."

"This might be one of [the] most embarrassing things i've ever seen a sports team say," posted another.

A third wrote, "I'm not positive but i think this statement just set [the] NHL back like 35 years."

Hellebuyck, 33, played his first 11 NHL seasons with Winnipeg. He had four years remaining on his seven-year contract when the Jets sent him to Newark.

The Devils are 5-0-0 in games started by Hellebuyck, who made 23 saves in Sunday's 4-0 win in Toronto.

Hellebuyck is scheduled to address the media Tuesday, and he said he will explain his trade demand then.

#2764. Lakers getting Luka Dončić back, but other big three member status uncertain

ATLANTA — The Lakers are getting their superstar guard back for Monday's road game against the Hawks.

But the status of his backcourt co-star was uncertain as of six hours before tipoff.

Luka Doncic scored a season-high 53 points in Friday's win over the Trail Blazers before sitting out of the Lakers' win over the Pistons on Sunday. AP Photo/Mark J. Terrill

Luka Dončić was taken off of the injury report ahead of the Lakers' matchup against the Hawks after sitting out of Sunday's 121-116 win over the Pistons because of low back tightness.

Austin Reaves stepped up in light of Dončić's absence, scoring a season-high 43 points to lead the Lakers to their fifth straight win and putting them at 9-2 entering Monday.

Austin Reaves stepped up against the Pistons in light of Luka Doncic not being available. Getty Images

Reaves was listed as questionable on the team's injury report against the Hawks because of an illness.

Quentin Grimes, who started in place of Dončić against the Pistons, was ruled out against the Hawks because of a sprained finger on his right hand.

Quentin Grimes seems to have avoided a major injury. NBAE via Getty Images

Coach JJ Redick said Grimes banged his finger during the game agains the Pistons, leading to the 6-foot-5 guard going back to the locker room during the fourth quarter on Sunday with a member of the medical staff. Redick said Grimes' X-rays came back negative.

Dončić is the league's leading scorer, averaging 40.1 points per game entering Monday.

#2763. 2. Bundesliga: Magdeburg thrash St. Pauli 4-1 to move up to 4th in the table

The hosts made a bright start and were rewarded for their early pressure in the 27th minute. Following a short corner routine involving Baris Atik and Alexander Nollenberger, Torben Müsel played the ball into the path of Mateusz Żukowski, who unleashed a powerful effort from distance into the top-right corner to give Magdeburg the lead.

St. Pauli struggled to find a response before the interval, with Magdeburg looking the more dangerous side. The visitors had opportunities of their own, but the hosts went into the break with a deserved advantage.

Magdeburg wasted little time extending their lead after the restart, with Anselmo García MacNulty making it 2-0 in the 48th minute. The defender found the net with a left-footed effort to put the hosts firmly in control.

However, St. Pauli responded ten minutes later when Eric Smith pulled one back in the 58th minute. The defender rose highest to head home Branimir Hrgota’s delivery, giving the visitors renewed hope of getting something from the encounter.

The visitors subsequently looked to build on their momentum, but Magdeburg remained organised and managed to keep their opponents at bay as the game entered its closing stages.

Any hopes of a St. Pauli comeback were ended in the 89th minute when Herbert Bockhorn restored Magdeburg’s two-goal advantage. Roko Simić then added further gloss to the scoreline with another late strike, completing a convincing 4-1 victory for the hosts.

The result represents another important three points for Petrik Sander’s side, who continue to establish themselves among the promotion contenders. St. Pauli, meanwhile, have now suffered consecutive league defeats following last weekend’s 3-1 loss to Nürnberg.

#2762. Fantasy Football 2026: Waiver Wire, week 10 edition

Welcome to the Fantasy Football Waiver Wire for Week 10.

We're at the halfway point of the NFL season, and for most fantasy leagues, only five weeks remain before the playoffs start. A critical stretch of the season is here, and with the bye weeks still in play and lots of players battling injuries, the Waiver Wire continues to be an important resource, even if it's mostly been picked clean in the last month or so.

The NFL trade deadline is Tuesday at 4 p.m., and whether certain players (Jonathan Taylor, Tony Pollard, George Pickens, Josh Downs, Brian Thomas, Jr., Dallas Goedert, and a few others) get shipped will have an impact on waiver moves. Pay close attention to trade news.

The Process: Each week, I try to identify the top Waiver Wire targets and evaluate the short- and long-term prospects of those players. I also do my best to reveal which players who are coming off a big week are fool's gold. Finally, I look at streamers who might help you in a pinch (this is usually more important during Bye weeks).

Some players discussed won't be available in your league. I try to list players who are available in more than 50% of leagues. But what does that even mean? A 10-team league with six bench spots will have a much larger Free Agent pool than a 14-team league with eight. Most leagues fall somewhere in between. This isn't "one size fits all." By way of example, I won't list Bryce Young, Kirk Cousins, Tyler Allgeier, Devin Singletary, Jordyn Tyson, or Ryan Flournoy, among others who probably aren't available in any of your leagues.

Note that I'm writing this column BEFORE the Week 9 Monday night game (MIN@BUF).

WEEK 10 BYES: Bears, Broncos, Buccaneers, and Eagles

Geno Smith makes my list once again. I think that makes five straight weeks. He struggled against the Chiefs, as expected, in a game played without Garrett Wilson (concussion). The Bills are up next, and Geno will be forced to play catch-up in that contest. The Chargers and Dolphins follow. Geno continues to be a viable play as a QB2.

Aaron Rodgers has been decent for fantasy this season and a mainstay in my QB waivers list. Coming off the bye this week, the Steelers get the red-hot, high-scoring Bengals. Rodgers threw for almost 300 yards and three TDs against Cincinnati in Week 6. He should be getting some more receiving help soon, and is a nice streaming option this week and beyond.

The QB2 options have thinned out due to injuries and poor play. Here's a telling statistic: 47 QBs have started games through Week 9 and that's the second highest total this century. There are quite a few available "starters" that I wouldn't look to add, either because they don't offer much upside (Joe Milton III, J.J. McCarthy, Gardner Minshew II, any QB on the Chargers) and/or it's not clear how long they'll be the starter (Shedeur Sanders, Drew Lock).

I'm curious to see how Carson Wentz plays on Monday night, and he could be one to consider if you're desperate. I have him ranked ahead of the guys I just listed.

Note that McCarthy and Lock are not locks to be healthy enough to play this week.

Need a Week 10 streamer? Jacoby Brissett might be available in your league. I'm not a big believer, but he should be ready to start for the Ravens in Week 10 and if you're stuck, you could do worse than a date with the Chargers, who gave zero effort on defense against the Texans.

I don't think the Raiders are trading Kirk Cousins or benching him any time soon. Stashing Fernando Mendoza isn't worth the roster spot at this point.

The trade deadline figures to have the biggest impact here. Seth McGowan is apparently still available in more than half of leagues on some big platforms. If Jonathan Taylor gets moved, his value shoots up. Even if he stays put, McGowan is getting more work and is one of the better handcuffs to roster.

The same is true for Tyjae Spears and Nick Singleton on the Titans, if Tony Pollard gets traded.

Corey Kiner stands to be the Week 10 starter for the Patriots if Rhamondre Stevenson misses time. Stevenson left Sunday's blowout loss to the Packers with a knee injury (plus, he lost a fumble), and TreVeyon Henderson missed the game with an illness. The Patriots only have one game before their Week 11 bye, so I wouldn't go too crazy with a bid for Kiner, but he's going to be a very popular add this week.

Audric Estime is another back who could get more run as the de facto "starter", but it could be short-lived, depending on when Travis Etienne, Jr., is back. Estime ran well as the "early downs" back on Sunday (15-72-1), while Alvin Kamara got most of the third down and passing game work. He'll be a competitive add this week, as this is a good offense and Etienne's imminent return isn't assured. This week's matchup (CAR) is a juicy one.

Kaleb Johnson and Marshawn Lloyd are worth adding, with Josh Jacobs possibly headed to IR. Green Bay can't run the ball, but volume is volume. Lloyd missed Week 9 with a knee injury so I'd prioritize Johnson.

Backs who should be rostered: Kenneth Gainwell, Dylan Sampson, Emmett Johnson, and Kaelon Black.

Jonathon Brooks (IR stash) is another to consider if you have an IR spot.

DeMario Douglas is filling the void created by A.J. Brown and Romeo Doubs being out of the lineup, and he's my top choice at WR. The volume is inconsistent, but he's quietly put up three Top-10 weekly finishes at his position over the past five weeks.

I'll recommend another guy with the same last name. Miami's passing offense is bad, but Caleb Douglas is emerging as their top target, and a decent downfield threat. He's put up between 70-95 receiving yards in three of four complete games he's played.

Kayshon Boutte and Tank Dell both went off in the Texans' rout this week, but I'd be careful chasing those performances too hard. Both receivers are boom/bust deep threats, and the Chargers didn't show up at all this week.

The 49ers and Rams continue to lose receivers like I lose my eyeglasses. I always eventually find them, though. These two teams have struggled to find replacements who can step up. It's hard to recommend much here, but I think you can consider Jordan Watkins, De'Zhaun Stribling (IR stash), and with Puka Nacua now joining Davante Adams on IR, one of Konata Mumpfield or Tutu Atwell is likely to step up (and keep an eye on the Rams trying to add a receiver via trade).

Need depth? I'll repeat a bunch of names I've listed in recent weeks, together with some new ones: Antonio Williams, Pat Bryant, Keenan Allen, Jahan Dotson, Jakobi Meyers, Savion Williams, and Chimere Dike.

Mike Gesicki finally didn't score last week. He's very TD-or-bust, and so is Pat Freiermuth. If you need a replacement this week, I like both as streaming options (their teams face each other).

Darren Waller went off in Week 9, after vanishing for more than a month, so he'll be a popular target this week. It's buyer beware, but an 8-90-1 line on 10 targets will always turn heads. Just know that you might get one catch for four yards next week.

Chig Okonkwo has been in this column for a solid month now. He hasn't popped but has been serviceable.

Terrance Ferguson was expected back last week but wasn't activated. Hopefully his ankle will be OK this week, as the Rams will be without Nacua and Adams for at least the next month. Ferguson will compete with the other tight ends in L.A., but when all is said and done, he should be the best of the bunch, in an offense that's going to have to feature them.

That's all I've got. Good luck with your waiver claims!

#2760. Game Preview: Phoenix looks to carry its defensive momentum into matchup with San Antonio

Who: Phoenix Suns (3-5) vs. San Antonio Spurs (8-2)

Where: Mortgage Matchup Center — Phoenix, Arizona

The fourth week of the NBA season kicks off Monday night as the Phoenix Suns open a back-to-back by welcoming Victor Wembanyama and the San Antonio Spurs to town for their only visit to Phoenix this season.

The Suns are coming off easily their most impressive victory of the season, having held a potent Houston Rockets offense to fewer than 100 points en route to a much-needed win. While the offense has begun showcasing greater connectivity and accountability, it's the defense that has truly stood out over the past two games. Since last Thursday, Phoenix has averaged 11.0 steals per game, the second most in the NBA during that span.

If the Suns hope to knock off a formidable Western Conference opponent in the defending conference champion Spurs, that defensive intensity will need to continue. San Antonio opened the season with consecutive losses before responding by rattling off eight straight victories, establishing itself as one of the hottest teams in basketball. The Spurs are playing with confidence, executing on both ends of the floor, and beginning to look every bit like the contender many expected them to be.

That being said, Phoenix has been something of a thorn in San Antonio's side over the past few seasons. Since 2023, the Spurs hold a narrow 6-5 advantage in the head-to-head series, illustrating that the Suns have been more than capable of making life difficult for Wembanyama and company.

If Phoenix can carry the defensive energy and collective effort it displayed against Houston into Monday night's matchup, perhaps it can find a way to make things uncomfortable for San Antonio once again.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Jordan Goodwin — QUESTIONABLE (Right Calf Soreness)

Jayden Quaintance — OUT (Right Knee Surgery)

Devin Vassell — OUT (Left Adductor Tightness)

How can you not have your eyes on Victor Wembanyama in this matchup? The guy is an absolutely domineering presence on the floor, capable of shrinking the court defensively while continuing to expand his offensive skill set with each passing season. Now in his fourth year, he already has a Defensive Player of the Year award on his résumé, and he's making a legitimate case for MVP consideration.

So how exactly do the Suns go about neutralizing one of the premier talents in the NBA? Or is the better strategy to focus their energy on the players surrounding him?

Perhaps the answer lies in limiting the damage inflicted by De'Aaron Fox, Stephon Castle, and Dylan Harper, forcing Wembanyama to shoulder as much of the offensive burden as possible. After all, think back to last week, when Wembanyama delivered one of the most absurd statistical performances we've ever witnessed, pouring in 52 points, grabbing 24 rebounds, and blocking eight shots. And the Spurs won that game by one point.

That's what makes this matchup so fascinating. Even when Wembanyama puts together a performance that looks like something generated by a video game with the difficulty turned all the way down, San Antonio isn't necessarily guaranteed to dominate. There may be an opportunity for Phoenix to survive an extraordinary individual performance if it can prevent the supporting cast from finding its rhythm.

So what I'll be watching tonight is the defensive philosophy Jordan Ott and his staff employ against Wembanyama. Do they dedicate additional resources to containing him, knowing that doing so could create opportunities for San Antonio's other offensive weapons? Or do they accept that Wembanyama is going to get his and concentrate on neutralizing the players around him?

Rebounding, right? Yes, that's generally my default mode. But if I want to dig a little deeper into this matchup, I think three-point shooting will play a significant role if the Suns hope to come away with a victory.

Given Wembanyama's affinity for denying opponents at the basket and his ability to quite literally change the geometry of the game, I expect Phoenix to be firing away from beyond the arc. That's going to present a challenge when Oso Ighodaro is on the floor, because every time he catches the ball above the break, Wembanyama can comfortably sag into drop coverage, knowing he doesn't have to respect Oso as a three-point threat.

While Ighodaro has been a welcome addition to the Suns' starting lineup, his offensive strengths, particularly his effectiveness in dribble handoff actions, become more difficult to utilize when Wembanyama doesn't necessarily have to involve himself in those actions defensively.

So can the Suns consistently generate quality three-point opportunities, and more importantly, can they knock them down? If the answer to both questions is yes, Phoenix has a legitimate chance to win this game.

The Spurs are good. Really good. And it's difficult for me to envision a scenario in which the Suns win this game. What I'm hoping to see is Phoenix maintain a competitive pace and build on what it accomplished against the Houston Rockets, further solidifying an identity that has been defined by early-season inconsistency.

If the Suns can impose aspects of their will, play physically against a San Antonio team that possesses some sizable guards, and remain competitive throughout the game, I'll view that as progress in and of itself. Not a moral victory, but another step in the right direction for a team still trying to prove it can perform with consistency.

But when all is said and done, I expect San Antonio to leave Phoenix with the win.

#2757. Michael Carrick on the brink at Manchester United amid club review

Michael Carrick's days as Manchester United head coach appear to be numbered as the club reviews his position and considers alternatives.

The Red Devils will spend the international break in the relegation zone after failing to add to August's solitary Premier League win against Ipswich in Sunday's 4-4 draw with Aston Villa.

United languish in 18th spot and are reviewing Carrick's position as the club take stock during the international break, the Press Association understands.

Reports suggest the club intend to replace the former midfielder following talks on Monday, although Old Trafford sources insisted no decision had been made that evening.

United director of football Jason Wilcox and chief executive Omar Berrada seriously considered sacking Carrick a fortnight ago after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth compounded a calamitous 4-1 Champions League loss at Como.

The club eventually decided to give the head coach and his players the opportunity to turn the situation around before the international break.

Carrick's side subsequently lost 2-0 at Chelsea, where they were unable to take their chances and saw Lisandro Martinez sent off, before last Tuesday's 3-2 comeback win against Roma and Sunday's 4-4 draw with Villa.

United have been considering whether that run merits him staying on and would have to push a manager who headed into the international break having stressed he would not walk away.

Asked if the November pause comes at a good time for the group, the under-fire United boss said: "Yeah, it's been a right mixed week or two or three, really, if you're looking at the events.

"But I'm pretty clear where we need to go and how we need to get there.

"Obviously some of the boys will be flying off around the world and different things, and the group will be split for 10 days or so.

"But as a normal international week is, you know, we'll have some training. We'll have one or two days off, but not too much. But we'll train, and we'll get ready for the next game."

The final international break of the year would seem a logical time if United wanted to make a change, given there is no free midweek until mid-December after returning with a trip to rivals Liverpool on November 22.

Former Newcastle boss Eddie Howe and ex-Germany head coach Julian Nagelsmann are contenders to replace Carrick if the club decide a change in the Old Trafford hotseat is necessary.

Mauricio Pochettino, the former Tottenham, Paris St Germain and Chelsea manager, has often been linked with the role and bookmakers have slashed odds on him coming in.

The former Argentina defender extended his deal in charge of the United States national team until 2030 just three months ago.

Carrick's future remains up in the air but the head coach is steadfast in his belief that he can lead the embattled side up the standings having seen enough positives since the nadir of Bournemouth.

"Goals change games, and you've got to deal with it, and it's part of something we have to improve on drastically," Carrick said.

"But there's more than enough. We're just making it too difficult for ourselves at the moment in certain times."

#2756. 2026-27 NHL season predictions: Expert picks for Stanley Cup playoffs, champion and major awards this season

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Following a brief offseason and an expedited preseason, the 2026-27 NHL season has arrived. Before the puck drops on opening night, we have some predictions to make.

If this season feel like it's staring earlier than usual, that's because it is. The NHL is adopting an 84-game regular season, which just means more hockey for the rest of us. That also means a long and arduous journey to the Stanley Cup is ever so slightly more difficult.

So, what will that journey look like? The Carolina Hurricanes are looking to defend their title as Cup champions, but they'll have to contend with a deep Eastern Conference and some real juggernauts in the Western Conference. With that in mind, we logged our expert predictions for the playoff field in each conference -- and there were a few surprises -- before making our Stanley Cup Final selections.

We also called our shot on the winners of each major individual award. Connor McDavid is looking for his fourth Hart Trophy, but he's got some competition from an up-and-coming superstar in his own division. Races for the Norris Trophy and Vezina Trophy should be pretty wide open, and unexpected candidates always emerge throughout the season.

It should be a fun season in the NHL, so follow along with all our coverage at CBS Sports. Let's get things kicked off with the season preview and predictions, which will of course be 100% accurate.

Baumgartner: I don't see many new faces entering the conversation when it comes to the Eastern Conference playoff race. A healthy Florida Panthers side will slot themselves right back towards the top of the Atlantic, and a revamped Washington Capitals side should be able to make some noise in the Metropolitan. Besides that, I have six 2026 playoff teams returning to the dance in 2027.

Nivison: As much as I hate to go with chalk in preseason predictions -- because it rarely happens that way -- there's no denying the top dogs in the West. The Avalanche and Stars are the class of the Central Division, and the Golden Knights should pound a pitiful Pacific Division into a pulp. Things get more interesting behind those clear Cup contenders, and I'm projecting another significant leap forward from the Mammoth. I also think Macklin Celebrini will be successful in dragging the Sharks to the playoffs in his third NHL season.

Nivison -- Columbus Blue Jackets: The Blue Jackets have been agonizingly close to the playoffs in each of the last two seasons, and I think they get over the hump in 2026-27. There are still questions in Columbus. Can Adam Fantilli be a franchise player at center? Is the forward group as a whole good enough to keep pace in a competitive Eastern Conference? Still, I am sure the Blue Jackets have a really strong blue line, led by Norris Trophy winner Zach Werenski, and goaltender Jet Greaves is on track to become a Vezina Trophy candidate. Internal improvements from the young core will then get Columbus above the cut line.

Baumgartner -- San Jose Sharks: The Sharks narrowly missed out on the 2026 Stanley Cup Playoffs, finishing just four points short of the second wild card. San Jose added a good group of veterans over the summer, but the most important offseason move was selecting Ivar Stenberg with the No. 2 pick in the Draft. Stenberg joins the youth movement in San Jose, which is spearheaded by new captain Macklin Celebrini, who broke the Sharks single-season points record with 115 last season. The Sharks are a better team than they were last year, and with Celebrini entering his third season as a legit Hart Trophy contender, this team won't be fun to play against.

Nivison: Yes, the Mammoth have a brutal path coming out of the Central Divison, but just close your eyes and imagine this scenario with me. Logan Cooley becomes a superstar center. Dylan Guenther hits 50 goals. Dmitry Simashev asserts himself as a budding top-four defenseman. Anders Lee and Vincent Trocheck provide necessary veteran depth. Now open your eyes. Utah is your Western Conference champion.

Unfortunately for the Mammoth, they will run into a refreshed Panthers team that now has double-barreled Tkachuks. Last season, we found out that all it takes to stop Florida is to hope all of its key players miss significant time with injury. The long offseason was probably a blessing in disguise as it allowed the Panthers to get healthy, and that's bad news for everyone else. The structure in front of the crease will limit some of the volatility we saw from Jacob Markstrom in New Jersey, and Florida wins its third Stanley Cup in four years.

Baumgartner: Before last season, the Carolina Hurricanes' kryptonite was the Eastern Conference Final. The Hurricanes were able to overcome their demons and win the East before ultimately taking home the franchise's first Stanley Cup since 2006. In the West, the Dallas Stars have been running into the same problem. After three straight seasons ending in the Western Conference Final, the Stars regressed last postseason, flaming out in the first-round to the Minnesota Wild. The Central Division will once again be a gauntlet, but I'm ready for Dallas to take the next step and reach the Stanley Cup Final.

While Dallas has continued to fall short in recent years, the Panthers have done the opposite. The Panthers made three consecutive Stanley Cup Final appearances, winning back-to-back Cups in 2023-24 and 2024-25. Last season, Florida's three-peat attempt came to a halt on day one of training after captain Aleksander Barkov went down with a non-contact injury, tearing both the ACL and MCL in his right knee. An offseason of healing for Barkov, and the rest of the banged up Panthers, has the team refreshed entering September for the first time in years. With nearly the entire 2025 Stanley Cup winning core still on the team and Brady Tkachuk added to the fold, Florida looks ready to win its third Stanley Cup in four seasons.

Nivison: Celebrini should have won the Hart Trophy last season, but for some incomprehensible reason, he wasn't even a finalist. This year, Celebrini replicates that performance and gets San Jose to the postseason to take home his first MVP award at age 20. Hughes posts a career year in his first full season with the Wild, and Spencer Carberry gets his flowers for winning the Metro Division after missing the playoffs last year.

Baumgartner: McDavid's ability to take over a game was evident nearly every time he touched the ice last season. His 24 points (11 goals,13 assists) in the final 14 games of the regular season -- without Leon Draisaitl in the lineup -- was one of the many reasons I put him No. 1 on my Hart Trophy ballot. With just two years left on his current contract, desperation may start to kick in to get the job in Edmonton, which would lead to another MVP-caliber season. Barkov is healthy and the frontrunner to win his fourth Selke, and Dahlin will have another strong season to take home his first career Norris Trophy.

#2755. Walker Kessler’s ‘relentless’ rebounding is proving vital for Lakers

Only a handful of players around the league are rebounding the ball better than Walker Kessler this season. On a per-game basis, he ranks sixth in total rebounds, sixth in offensive rebounds and 11th in defensive rebounds. When he's on the floor, the Los Angeles Lakers' offensive rebounding rate is 6.9% better than when he sits, according to Cleaning the Glass.

During Sunday's impressive road victory over the Detroit Pistons, Kessler gobbled up 16 rebounds, seven of which came on the offensive glass. Over his last six games, he's collected 28 offensive rebounds. On a team full of talented on- and off-ball shot-makers, Kessler is helping give the Lakers more bites at the apple offensively.

"Seven offensive rebounds. The last six, seven games, he's just been relentless with keeping possessions alive for us," head coach JJ Redick told reporters Sunday. "I thought he was awesome. I thought he was awesome protecting the rim. … I love the consistency right now for him. He's like, 'I'm going to protect this rim and do it over and over and over again.' He had a big and one there in the fourth."

Kessler's been a pretty prolific offensive rebounder since he debuted in 2022, averaging 3.4 per game for his career. In 2024-25 — his last fully healthy season — he led the NBA in both offensive rebounds per game (4.6) and offensive rebounding rate (16.6%). He recognizes his 7-foot-2 frame helps him excel but makes clear there's much more to it than simply being tall and believes he has another level to reach — and will.

"Obviously with my size, that definitely helps, but definitely a lot of it is about timing," Kessler said Sunday, when he chipped in 12 points, four assists, three blocks and one steal alongside his 16 boards. "A lot of it is about hitting the guy first, not getting pushed onto the rim and I got to do a better job of those balls that I get my hands on, securing the ball. But that comes with time and my hands get back normal, that'll be good as well."

#2754. Grading the trade: Jaguars making run at Super Bowl after landing Eagles DT Jordan Davis

The Jacksonville Jaguars made their third trade on Monday, and it was the biggest one yet — literally and figuratively.

The Eagles will ship massive defensive tackle Jordan Davis (6-foot-6, 336 pounds) to the Jaguars. In return, Jacksonville will fork over a 2027 first-round draft pick and a 2029 second-round selection to Philadelphia.

Earlier Monday, the Jaguars acquired safety Jessie Bates III from the Atlanta Falcons and defensive tackle Davon Godchaux from the New Orleans Saints.

How does Davis fit into the Jaguars' defense? What about the haul they got in return? Let's break down this massive trade.

If you thought the Bates trade was a sign that the Jaguars were going for it, the Davis trade left no doubt.

When DaVon Hamilton went down with a season-ending injury, it was viewed as a massive loss. Hamilton might not be a household name outside of Jacksonville, but he was valued highly inside the team's facility.

So what did the Jaguars do? They went out and got two defensive tackles to plug that hole — nearly 700 combined pounds between them. Think that will plug the hole up front?

The Jaguars might still have a top-10 rushing defense this season, but the Baltimore Ravens ran for a season-high 172 yards against them in their first game without Hamilton. That the game happened Thursday night likely allowed the Jaguars to recalibrate their needs over the weekend and strike quickly Monday. Three times, in fact.

They were not getting major interior help from the likes of Arik Armstead, Ruke Orhorhoro, Matt Dickerson and Albert Regis, and that's why the Jaguars added multiple players up front. The appeal of Godchaux is that he can play in multiple spots, and Davis also can move around a little, although they figure to hold down the nose between them. That likely leaves Armstead and Orhorhoro as the 3-techniques.

If you look at the Jaguars' remaining schedule, there aren't a lot of strong running teams left on it. However, if we're using the all-in framework, this deal was as much about what the Jaguars might face in the playoffs. Three of the top four teams by rush average reside in the AFC — the Pittsburgh Steelers, Buffalo Bills and Kansas City Chiefs — and the Jaguars might have to go through at least two of them to get to the Super Bowl.

Now comes the cost, and it's robust. Next year's first-round pick is likely to be in the bottom half of Round 1. The Jaguars hope it's the 32nd pick, which goes to the Super Bowl winner; if the season ended today, they'd be giving up the 25th overall selection. We can't project where the 2029 second-rounder will land, but teams often subtract one round for every year in the future, which makes it about a fourth-rounder for now.

When you put it like that, the cost doesn't sound quite as high, even if there are some conditions attached. Still, you have to appreciate a team willing and able to strike when the irons are hot.

The flip side: Davis has been ordinary this season — and at times invisible. The Jaguars clearly feel they can unlock his talent, or they wouldn't have paid so much. Lining up on a unit that features Josh Hines-Allen and Travon Walker should help, but Davis' paltry production (0.5 sacks, two QB hits, no forced fumbles or tackles for loss and a snap rate hovering around 50%) is concerning.

The Eagles signed Davis to a three-year, $78 million extension in March, yet Davis' salary cap hit for 2027 is a very reasonable figure at just over $8.5 million. This is an important element for the deal, with the Jaguars relatively tight against the projected cap for next spring. There's plenty of time to make it all work down the road.

For now, the Jaguars have their sights set on a Super Bowl.

The Eagles had let it be known that Moro Ojomo, the team's talented third-year defensive tackle, was not available for trade. And they kept to their word.

Instead, they dealt Davis, whom the team signed to a major extension along with Jalen Carter this offseason. The thinking was that Davis and Carter would be the starting pillars up front for years to come. That now has come to an end, but a Carter-Ojomo pairing now sounds appealing, especially with Davis performing at a disappointing level this season.

In addition to shedding salary, the Eagles also have continued adding to their impressive draft-pick nest egg. In 2027, they now own the Jaguars' first-rounder, the Vikings third-rounder, plus other teams' Day 3 picks, in addition to most of their own selections. In the 2028 draft, they own the Patriots' first-rounder and the Ravens' second-rounder. And now they're adding 2029 selections to the till.

With Jalen Hurts' career hanging in the balance following his blood clot diagnosis, the Eagles need that flexibility in case they must acquire another quarterback. That's the reality. Even if Hurts is eventually cleared to play, there's some long-term doubt about whether he remains the face of the franchise.

With the Eagles showing a willingness to deal assets, it appears that they're fully throwing in the towel on the playoffs, even with the NFC East nominally still up for grabs. That also impacts Nick Sirianni's future as he now coaches a team that has gone from possible Super Bowl contenders to more talent-poor in a hurry, especially given all their injury trouble.

It's a tough short-term pill to swallow for the franchise and a fan base used to competing every season, but the long-term payoff could be immense. The Eagles' to-do list this offseason grows, but the potential of how they can reshape the roster is equally as exciting.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2753. Reds' Sal Stewart wins NL Rookie of the Year after 112 RBI season

Sal Stewart never took a day off this season. He played all 162 games for the Cincinnati Reds, hit 32 homers and drove in 112 runs. No Reds rookie had ever driven in more.

Monday, it made him the National League Rookie of the Year.

"Man, relieved," Stewart said on MLB Network after winning the award. "I told myself I knew this award was going to find me, because that's how I was raised. I worked hard and I did what I could.

"I'm a kid from Miami. I worked hard for this moment. A lot of people doubted me."

The 22-year-old first baseman received 20 of 30 first-place votes and 170 points from the Baseball Writers' Association of America. New York Mets outfielder Carson Benge was second with seven first-place votes and St. Louis Cardinals second baseman JJ Wetherholt was third with three.

Stewart is the ninth Reds player to win the award. Jonathan India was the last in 2021.

Cincinnati gets a draft pick out of it. MLB's Prospect Promotion Incentive rewards teams that give top prospects a full rookie season in the majors, and the NL winner earns his club an extra pick after the first round of the 2027 draft.

Stewart spent most of the summer as the best run producer in the sport. On Aug. 26, he became the first player in the majors to reach 100 RBIs. He finished third in baseball, behind Alec Burleson's 116 and Pete Alonso's 113.

2018 AL – P/DH Shohei Ohtani, Los Angeles Angels

2018 AL – P/DH Shohei Ohtani, Los Angeles Angels

By the end of April, Stewart had nine homers and 29 RBIs, was hitting .281 and had gone 7-for-7 stealing bases.

May and June were harder. He hit under .250 in both months.

He fixed it in July and was named an All-Star. In August, he won NL Rookie of the Month for the second time, the first Reds player to win it twice in one season.

The Reds kept finding spots to keep his bat and speed in the lineup. Stewart appeared in 117 games at first base, 45 at third, 11 as the designated hitter and eight at second base.

Jim Greengrass drove in 100 runs as a Reds rookie in 1953. His record held up for 73 years.

Frank Robinson hit 38 homers as a rookie in 1956. Until this year, he was the only Reds rookie with 30. Stewart is the first to do it with 100 RBIs. His 20 homers before the All-Star break were another franchise record.

He finished with 164 hits, tied for second in the NL, the most by a Reds rookie since Pete Rose had 170 in 1963. He led all NL rookies in homers, RBIs, extra-base hits (57) and total bases (285).

No Reds rookie had played every game since the NL moved to a 162-game schedule.

Only one other rookie has put up 30 homers, 100 RBIs and 15 steals in a season: Jose Canseco in 1986.

Pat Zachry, pitcher, 1976 (shared with San Diego's Butch Metzger)

Not everyone thought Stewart was the best rookie in the league.

Stewart had the power numbers. Benge and Wetherholt had the better all-around seasons.

Benge hit .282 with 20 homers, 65 RBIs and 22 steals for the Mets. He was the better hitter for average, the faster runner and better fielder.

Wetherholt, the Cardinals' second baseman, didn't come close to Stewart at the plate. He hit .242 with 17 homers. But he was a Gold Glove finalist and one of the best defenders in the league.

That's why both rated well ahead of Stewart in WAR. FanGraphs had Benge at 4.3 and Stewart at 1.9. Baseball Reference had Wetherholt at 4.5 and Stewart at 1.8. Stewart's defense at first and third base cost him.

Stewart hit .247 over his final 24 games, with one extra-base hit and six RBIs. Alec Burleson passed him for the NL lead in RBIs in the final week.

The voters still went with the homers, the RBIs and the 162 games.

2025: Drake Baldwin, catcher, Atlanta Braves

2024: Paul Skenes, pitcher, Pittsburgh Pirates

2023: Corbin Carroll, outfielder, Diamondbacks*

2022: Michael Harris II, outfielder, Atlanta Braves

2021: Jonathan India, second baseman, Cincinnati Reds

2020: Devin Williams, pitcher, Milwaukee Brewers

2019: Pete Alonso, first baseman, New York Mets

2018: Ronald Acuna Jr., outfielder, Atlanta Braves

#2750. Nick Sirianni denies flipping off Eagles fans after home loss to Giants as skid continues: 'Absolutely not'

No, Nick Sirianni did not intentionally give Philadelphia Eagles fans the middle finger while walking into the tunnel at Lincoln Financial Field on Sunday afternoon.

The Eagles head coach said he was stunned when he was informed of that speculation following Philadelphia's 19-11 loss to the New York Giants on Sunday. That gesture, Sirianni insisted, was not something he intentionally did in the wake of what was a seventh straight loss.

"Absolutely not," Sirianni said, via ESPN's Tim McManus. "That was a surprise to me, but absolutely not."

It is easy to understand why Eagles fans may be frustrated with Sirianni. The Eagles (2-7) have lost seven straight games now, and sit last in the NFC East. They are all but out of the postseason race this fall, and the team looks incredibly different than the one that won the Super Bowl two seasons ago. They've lost quarterback Jalen Hurts due to blood clots, too, and have been struggling since.

Assuming the Eagles don't miraculously rally the rest of the way, this will be just the second time in the past decade that they didn't make the postseason. They've won two Super Bowl titles in that span.

On Sunday, as Sirianni left the field, his right hand was moving around a bit awkwardly as he walked past fans and into the tunnel. That led to speculation about the middle finger gesture, though Sirianni never looked anywhere but forward and definitely didn't make a clear gesture. His demeanor on the walk in question never changed.

Sirianni has a history of yelling at fans, including an incident in which he apologized after shouting at a group of Eagles fans during a 2024 game with the Cleveland Browns. But Sunday's exit from the field wasn't even close to that.

The Eagles are entering their bye week, and will return Nov. 22 for a matchup with the Pittsburgh Steelers. They dealt defensive tackle Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars on Monday, in return for a first-round draft pick next spring and a second-round selection in 2029. They now have a combined 10 picks in the first two rounds of the next three drafts, which should give them plenty of room to regroup after this season ends.

Until then, Sirianni will attempt to get the Eagles back on track during the final stretch of the season. He didn't rule out any coaching changes, either, but that's not his focus just yet.

"Right now we're going through the self-scout process," Sirianni said. "Guys are working on studies right now, and we're determined to figure things out and get on the right track, and that's what we're worried about right at this particular time."

#2749. Devin Vassell ruled out against Suns with adductor tightness

In a very young 2026-27 NBA season that has been rife with injuries, the Spurs have mostly managed to stay above the fray, with Tobias Harris being the only rotation player to miss time with a strained calf suffered in training camp. (And even without him, the Spurs still have the same team that made the NBA Finals.) Tonight will be the first time they are missing a main rotation player, with starting wing Devin Vassell initially listed as questionable before being ruled out against the Suns with adductor tightness.

Vassell joins Harris and Jayden Quaintance (knee surgery) on the injury report. Two-way players Ja'Kobi Gillespie and Maliq Brown are also away from the team with the Austin Spurs. Just like last season, Vassell has been an integral part of the Spurs' starting line-up not by putting up big numbers, but by being a steadying presence and reliable relief valve who can get a shot up in the midrange when things break down on offense. He has also been solid on defense despite often having to defend taller players while playing next to the guard trio.

This will mark the first time this season the Spurs will have to change the starting line-up. The safe assumption for his replacement would be Julian Champagnie, who was the Spurs starting power forward last season before moving to a bench role this season with Dylan Harper joining the starters. Hopefully Vassell's injury is nothing serious and he will return soon (the fact that he was initially questionable suggests it likely isn't serious, but adductors can be stubborn).

There has been no timeline offered for Harris' potential return, although Mitch Johnson has stated that they are being "very methodical" about it, especially considering the Spurs' fast pace. With an exponential rise in ruptured Achilles associated with playing on sore calves in recent years, who can blame them?

#2748. Sal Stewart named NL Rookie of the Year

Reds first baseman Sal Stewart has been named NL Rookie of the Year, taking home 20 of 30 first place votes en route to a comfortable win. Stewart takes home the award on the back of a .263/.330/.457 season (112 wRC+) with 32 HR and 112 RBI, with 15 steals tacked on to boot. Carson Benge finished second, while Nolan McLean finished 4th.

Benge was named a finalist for the award alongside JJ Wetherholdt and Stewart last week. Thomas Henderson profiled each of those finalists earlier today, concluding that Stewart was the likeliest to win on the back of his 30 homer season. Indeed, Stewart has already been named Sporting News Rookie of the Year, a version of award voted on by players rather than writers. Earlier today ahead of the official announcement, betting odds (per Kalshi) had Stewart as a shoe-in for the official version of the award.

While it's easy to understand why Stewart has supporters (Dingerz, baby), his analytical case is quite poor. Wetherholdt and Benge were both far superior players by all WAR metrics, and even other rookies like TJ Rumfield and Joe Mack were more valuable overall contributors. A.J. Ewing, who tallied only 492 AB to Stewarts's 700, was nearly as valuable in his own right. At least three rookies on the mound – Logan Henderson, Andrew Alvarez, and Nolan McLean – were also more valuable players.

In prior years, this would've been a mild annoyance to debate in chat threads or on Twitter, but with the implementation of the Prospect Promotion Incentive (PPI) system there are actual stakes here. For the Mets, this is a frustrating and somewhat damaging outcome. Despite having two of the three best rookies in Benge and McLean, the org will not receive anything in the way of draft pick awards, a boon that would've greatly helped their eternally challenged draft pool.

#2746. 2. Bundesliga: Bochum snatch dramatic late victory over Karlsruher SC

The hosts made an electric start and needed just six minutes to take the lead. Berkan Taz picked out Christian Rasmussen on the left-hand side of the penalty area, with the Danish forward taking aim and firing a superb effort into the far corner beyond Hans Christian Bernat.

However, Karlsruhe responded almost immediately. Just two minutes later, Marvin Wanitzek teed up Kevin Wiethaup on the edge of the penalty area, and the midfielder produced an equally impressive finish into the top corner to restore parity.

Bochum subsequently enjoyed more possession and looked the more threatening side, with Karol Mets going closest to restoring their advantage. The defender was unable to convert from close range after Philipp Hofmann had nodded the ball into his path, while Jarne Steuckers also tested Bernat with a powerful effort from outside the box.

Despite the hosts’ pressure, Karlsruhe remained organised and managed to keep the score level heading into the interval.

The visitors made a flying start to the second half and took the lead just five minutes after the restart. Following a Bochum corner, Shio Fukuda helped keep the ball alive before Tim Civeja spotted Timo Horn off his line and produced a sensational strike from around the halfway line, sending the ball over the goalkeeper and into the net to make it 2-1.

Bochum responded almost immediately, however, with Hofmann bringing the hosts level in the 53rd minute. After Taz delivered the ball into the penalty area, Rasmussen helped it into the path of the former Karlsruhe striker, who made no mistake from close range.

Both sides subsequently searched for a winner, with Bochum coming particularly close through Taz and Hofmann in the 74th minute. Bernat initially parried Taz’s effort before producing an outstanding reaction save to deny Hofmann from the rebound.

The Karlsruhe goalkeeper continued to frustrate the hosts as the game entered its closing stages, while Bochum also had to remain alert to the visitors’ counter-attacking threat.

However, Uwe Rösler’s side eventually found their breakthrough in the second minute of stoppage time. After Mansour Ouro-Tagba drove forward down the left, Hofmann helped the ball back into the path of Taz, whose effort was saved by Bernat. The resulting attack remained alive, and Karol Mets delivered the decisive ball into the penalty area for Hofmann to finish from close range and complete a remarkable comeback.

The late winner sparked celebrations around the Vonovia Ruhrstadion, with Bochum claiming all three points in an entertaining five-goal encounter.

The victory continues Bochum’s recent improvement under Rösler, while Karlsruhe will be left frustrated after twice taking the lead and ultimately coming away empty-handed.

#2737. Alexander Ovechkin announces this will be his last NHL season: 'Let's have fun'

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Alex Ovechkin's 22nd NHL season will be his last, the Washington Capitals superstar announced on Monday."This is gonna be my last year," Ovechkin told his teammates in a video the Capitals posted to social media. "It's sad, but it is what it is. We have an unbelievable team this year. There are 82 games left. Let's make the playoffs, and let's have fun."

Ovechkin has already established himself as the greatest goal-scorer in hockey history with 929 tallies, and he still has 82 games to add to that total before he hangs up his skates.

The No. 1 overall pick in the 2004 NHL Draft, Ovechkin immediately established himself as a prolific scorer by notching 52 goals en route to winning the Calder Trophy as a rookie.

Since then, Ovechkin has won the Rocket Richard Trophy as the NHL's leading goal-scorer a record nine times. The Capitals legend's resume also features an Art Ross Trophy and three Hart Trophy wins.

The crowning achievement in Ovechkin's illustrious career is leading Washington to its first Stanley Cup in 2017-18. After coming up short in a number of postseasons prior to that, Ovechkin led all playoff performers with 15 goals and won the Conn Smythe Trophy as a result.

Following the 2025-26 season, there was speculation that Ovechkin could retire, but he signed an incentive-laden one-year deal with the Caps.Sensing that this could be Ovechkin's final season, the Capitals loaded up to give their franchise icon one more shot at the Stanley Cup. Washington signed former Buffalo Sabres winger Alex Tuch in free agency and acquired winger Jordan Kyrou from the St. Louis Blues via trade.

No team is as loaded on the wings as the Capitals, and they have one of the best rosters in the Metro Division. Washington has the ingredients to challenge the defending Cup champion Carolina Hurricanes and get Ovechkin one more ring before he rides off into the sunset.

#2735. Who won Jordan Davis trade? Grading Jaguars, Eagles in deal

In a day defined by the Jacksonville Jaguars' pre-NFL trade deadline dealings, the team's last move proved most consequential of all.

After completing two additional deals earlier in the day to boost their defense, the Jaguars agreed to acquire defensive tackle Jordan Davis from the Philadelphia Eagles in exchange for a 2027 first-round pick and a 2029 second-round pick.

The move served as a loud statement of purpose for both franchises. For the Jaguars, it was the strongest push yet in an ongoing effort to equip an otherwise stellar roster with everything it needs to make a title push. The Eagles, meanwhile, appear to be preparing themselves for a potential reset after rampant injuries and an ongoing seven-game skid dashed any hope of the organization remaining in the NFC's contending class.

But who made out better in the deal? Grades for each team:

With Monday's earlier trades to secure safety Jessie Bates III from the Atlanta Falcons and defensive tackle Davon Godchaux from the New Orleans Saints, the Jaguars had already made clear their belief in this group's ability to run with the AFC's best. But those moves seemed more about isolated opportunities rather than changing the course of the franchise.

This decision, however, belongs in its own category. In burning serious draft capital to land a monstrous presence in the middle, general manager James Gladstone is harkening back to his roots and having a full Los Angeles Rams moment.

But is Davis the kind of player who truly puts Jacksonville over the top?

Gladstone likes to talk about players who have "gravity." At 6-6 and 336 pounds, Davis certainly qualifies as one. Trying to properly account for him while also keeping edge rushers Josh Hines-Allen and Travon Walker at bay will be exceedingly difficult for any opponent. And the Jaguars simply haven't enjoyed rolling out this kind of force in the middle for quite a while.

Still, defensive tackles who fetch this kind of price tag typically offer far more against the pass than Davis does. His 6.6% pressure rate, while close to a career high, is nowhere near the likes of Jeffery Simmons and the other elite disruptors at the position.

There's a strong case to be made that Davis can be a force multiplier for Anthony Campanile's unit, which has proven to be plenty opportunistic in sustaining itself with pressure and takeaways. But Jacksonville is now without its first two selections in next year's draft. The Rams fueled their outlier approach to top picks by continually finding starting-caliber options – particularly at non-premium positions – in the middle to later rounds of the draft. Gladstone hasn't shown himself capable of that just yet. And given the uncertain state of the offensive line, that hubris could one day take a serious toll on Jacksonville's roster.

Jacksonville might have arrived at the same conclusion that other teams have in determining that a first-round pick can be worth the instantaneous returns from landing a top-tier defensive tackle. The Jaguars certainly paid as though they're getting such a player, who's already making top-of-market money. It's far from a certainty, however, that the bottom line will match the spend.

Regardless of whether Roseman goes any further in parting with veteran pieces, this was a shrewd value move.

And when it comes to deciding whether to pull the plug early on a marquee player, few NFL decision-makers have a better track record than the architect of the Eagles.

Davis was a sensible option for Philadelphia to keep when it was at the front of the pack in the NFC. With the team fading considerably and staring down major changes in the near future, however, the 2022 first-round pick became expendable.

This is the kind of return one might expect for a borderline elite defensive lineman. Davis, however, likely wasn't among the three most critical defenders on the Eagles' roster.

In the next three draft classes, Roseman is now equipped with 10 picks in the first two rounds. That gives him incredible flexibility to target a quarterback of the future as he sees fit and fast-track a return to relevance.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Who won Jordan Davis trade? Grading Jaguars, Eagles in deal

#2734. Vikings-Bills live discussion: A chaotic matchup between a pair of overrated teams

The 6-1 Buffalo Bills take on the 6-1 Minnesota Vikings. These are two of the best teams in the NFL record-wise, but they are from the most complete teams.

The Vikings' offense has been asleep at the wheel all season. Justin Jefferson is on pace for a career worst year. He won't have Kyler Murray throwing him the ball tonight, but that might be a blessing in disguise, as Murray tends to be erratic and miss open receivers downfield.

What Minnesota lacks offensively, they more than make up for it on the defensive side of the ball. They are comfortably the best stop unit in the league, thanks in large part to their ability to prevent success against the pass. Brian Flores blitzes over 3/4 of the time, and invites chaotic play.

If there's a quarterback who thrives in chaos, it's Josh Allen. Allen makes play after play, usually off-script. He's down left tackle Dion Dawkins, which might invite Allen to run more. I can see Allen and James Cook having big days on the ground, and Khalil Shakir pitching in on key third downs.

The Bills are lousy defensively. They've allowed 33, 31, 29, 34, and 30 points this season. So, if there was a get-right spot for Carson Wentz, Jefferson, and the Vikings, it's the Bills' defense that gave up 30 points to the Raiders team we just watched.

My gut says the NFC's strength tops the AFC's in this game, and the Vikings eke out a win. But going against Allen is asking for it.

Deep down, I think these are two overrated teams that'll be exposed come playoff time.

#2729. Is the greatness of Warriors' Steph Curry being taken for granted?

It's rare that a player, especially a point guard, at 38 years of age has been as good as Stephen Curry has been.

For nearly two decades, the Golden State Warriors' superstar has wowed spectators, surprised teammates, and even coaches with his ability to take over games with a barrage of jaw-dropping, some head-scratching, heart-shattering shots.

Curry is in his 18th season, and what he's accomplished all-time has been nothing short of spectacular to start the year. His latest act featured a 41-point outing in a 126-119 overtime win against the Orlando Magic on Saturday, Nov. 7. He scored 17 of his 41 in the final 2:32 of the fourth quarter and overtime, lifting Golden State to its 7th consecutive win after a 0-3 start.

He's averaging 31.3 points per game through his first 8 games of the 2026–27 season. For however long it lasts, just don't take it for granted. Draymond Green doesn't and he's played with Curry since 2012. He tries to make a point to tell him congratulations for anything and everything he accomplishes.

"You work so hard to get into a position and then after you do something so many times, people don't even tell you congratulations anymore," Green said. "As human beings, you want to feel that ... so I try to make it a point no matter what he does. ... You want to hear other people acknowledge what you do. So I try to make it a point to never get kind of bored, if you will, of his greatness."

Head coach Steve Kerr said Curry looks like he's a 29- or 30-year-old, if he didn't already know his age. He's impressed with everything that goes into the longevity of Curry.

"Work ethic, and good genes," Kerr said. "He's been blessed with a body that has held up, but he's also earned that through his strength training, his conditioning, fitness level. It's just off the charts. So remarkable, at 38, to watch him still do what he does."

Curry has the most 40-point games (16) for a player age 35 or older. He ranks 19th all-time in points scored with 26,778 career points. Most recently, he became the 14th player in NBA history to accumulate at least 25,000 points, 5,000 rebounds, and 5,000 assists. He's only the third point guard ever to do so, joining Oscar Robertson and Russell Westbrook.

Curry knows that as he continues to play the game, he's going to climb all-time stat lines as well. It's a testament to the work he's put in over the years, which he said includes his day-to-day, the off-season, and sleep. Is it a lot? Yes. But Curry appreciates every step of the way.

"It does make me super proud of the work that's gone in," Curry said of his 40-point at age 35 or older record. "Especially with some injuries and all of that, but just the idea I can still get to a certain level (and) can kind of feed off of that and especially when it's in a winning fashion ... I appreciate the longevity on that."

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Is the greatness of Warriors' Steph Curry being taken for granted?

#2727. 2. Bundesliga: Heidenheim and Hannover share the points in four-goal thriller

The visitors made the brighter start and came close to opening the scoring inside the opening 15 minutes. Benjamin Källman did well to get in behind the Heidenheim defence before pulling the ball back for Husseyn Chakroun, who somehow missed the target with the goal at his mercy.

Hannover continued to look dangerous, with Marcel Hartel and Stefán Teitur Thórdarson also threatening, while Heidenheim struggled to create much of note in the opening stages.

Despite the visitors looking the more likely to score, it was Frank Schmidt’s side who broke the deadlock shortly before half-time. A long-range free-kick from Paul Hennrich caused problems inside the Hannover penalty area, with Jean Hugonet inadvertently diverting the ball into the path of Tim Siersleben, who reacted quickest to head home from close range in the 43rd minute.

Heidenheim therefore went into the interval with a somewhat fortunate 1-0 advantage, despite Hannover having created the better opportunities during the opening 45 minutes.

Sandro Wagner’s side came out with renewed purpose after the restart and were rewarded for their efforts in the 55th minute. Källman picked out Thórdarson on the edge of the penalty area, with the midfielder producing a precise first-time finish into the bottom-right corner to bring Hannover level.

The visitors continued to build momentum and completed the turnaround just eight minutes later. Marcel Hartel delivered a dangerous corner into the penalty area, where Hugonet rose highest to power a header beyond Thomas Dähne and put Hannover 2-1 ahead in the 63rd minute.

However, their advantage lasted less than three minutes. Heidenheim responded through a corner of their own, with substitute Christian Conteh delivering an inviting ball towards the near post for Budu Zivzivadze to glance a header into the net and restore parity at 2-2.

Both sides subsequently pushed for a winner in an increasingly open encounter. Hannover continued to threaten through Chakroun, while Heidenheim looked to take advantage of their attacking momentum following the equaliser.

Neither side could find a decisive breakthrough during the closing stages, however, with the points ultimately shared after an entertaining afternoon in Heidenheim.

The result extends Heidenheim’s winless run, while Hannover will take encouragement from their second-half response after coming from behind to take the lead, even if they were unable to hold on for all three points.

Manchester United coach Michael Carrick 'expected' to be fired after rough start appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Currently in the Premier League's relegation zone, Manchester United have won just one league game so far this season. Their eight points put them above Sunderland on goal difference alone, while both clubs are only one point ahead of last-place Crystal Palace. After saving the Red Devils in an interim role last year, head coach Michael Carrick had the label removed after the club's finish following former manager Ruben Amorim's firing.

Now, it appears that Carrick could be joining him. According to The Athletic's David Ornstein, Adam Crafton, and Laurie Whitwell, Manchester United is expected to fire Carrick before this current international break ends.

"Manchester United are expected to relieve Michael Carrick of his duties as head coach," wrote the trio on Monday afternoon.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

United's 1-5-4 record and -5 goal differential are just the start of the club's woes so far this fall. Following another busy summer transfer window in which the Red Devils' midfield was rebuilt with three signings, expectations were high at Old Trafford. Many believed that Manchester United would not only challenge for the Premier League title, but once again compete in European football as well.

However, Carrick has failed to deliver on those goals. Manchester United has only beaten Ipswich Town at home in the Premier League, and that was on August 30th. They've only won two of their four Champions League matchups, most recently against Roma at home last week. A home victory over Sabah on September 10th is the only other victory for the Red Devils this fall.

After a disappointing start filled with heartbreak and questionable decision making, it makes sense that the Manchester United brass would look to make a change. Owners Jim Ratcliffe and the Glazer Family, along with other key team leaders like team CEO Omar Berrada, will make the ultimate call on Carrick's future over the current international break.

By the time that United return to the pitch on November 22nd against Liverpool at Anfield, a new head coach could be roaming the visiting sidelines. Will Carrick's potential replacement lead Manchester United back to the promised land? Or will it be another failed appointment, like Carrick, Amorim and Erik ten Hag were over the last three years?

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

I hate to get so negative just one week into the season, but the Philadelphia Flyers have forced my hand. After a magical run to the second round of the 2026 Stanley Cup Playoffs, the Flyers have fallen on their face through four games.

The Flyers are off to an 0-2-2 start, and the underlying numbers aren't pretty. Per Natural Stat Trick, they have a five-on-five expected goals share of 26.0%, which ranks last in the NHL. Their 4.57 xGA/60 also ranks last. Their 1.94 xGF/60? You guessed it. Last.

2029: 1st, 2nd, Jaguars' 2nd (Jordan Davis)

So, yes, the Eagles will be reloading quickly.

#2717. Willie Fritz on expanded College Football Playoff and Cougars' chances this year | UH football notes

University of Houston coach Willie Fritz said Monday that he supports the expansion of the College Football Playoff to 24 teams, saying the new format will give more programs access to the postseason.

The CFP will double in size beginning in 2027, part of a five-year deal between ESPN and the CFP announced Monday, while also eliminating Power Four conference championship games.

"I think it gives more people access," Fritz said at his weekly press conference to preview Friday's game at Colorado. "I think people are thirsty for college football and quality games, and that will certainly give you more."

BOHLS: Expansion to 24 teams is wrong, greedy and bad for sport

The 24-team field will include 19 at-large bids, the Power Four champions as determined by each league, plus the highest-ranked Group of Six team.

The Big 12 announced Monday that this year's conference championship game, Dec. 4 in Arlington, will be its last. Beginning next year, the regular-season champion will receive the Big 12's automatic bid.

In a 24-team field, UH - which was No. 17 in the first CFP rankings - would be in good position to receive an at-large bid and be in position to host a first-round game on campus.

The Cougars are still in the hunt for this season's 12-team field, needing to win their final three games and have Texas Tech lose once. In that scenario, UH would win most of the tiebreakers involving multiple two-loss teams and advance to the Big 12 championship game.

The Cougars' current CFP ranking is the highest in program history.

"You want to be relevant," Fritz said. "We certainly want Houston to be out there front and center as much as possible. So that's great. But you've got to finish the year, and you've got to keep playing well. Being ranked right now doesn't mean a whole lot. You want to be ranked as high as we can at the end of the season. That means we've accomplished some things."

Linebacker Jaden Yates had a team-high nine tackles Saturday after missing a month with a lower-body injury.

Yates suffered the injury in Week 5 against UCF and missed the next three games before returning after the open week.

"I thought he did a good job," Fritz said. "He doesn't seem to have any side effects from the injury.

There are a lot of things that he does for us back there."

Yates has the green dot on the back of his helmet, which signifies he is equipped with a coach-to-player radio communication system. Part of Yates' responsibility is to relay instructions from defensive coordinator Austin Armstrong to the rest of the unit and help with alignment. When he was out, that responsibility fell to Sione Fotu and Carmycah Glass.

"I think (Yates) knocked a lot of the rust off and will play even better this week," Fritz said.

Linebacker Carmycah Glass was scheduled to be evaluated by team doctors Monday afternoon after leaving late in the first half against Cincinnati with a lower-body injury.

"It's not 100%," Fritz said. "I'll know a lot more tomorrow."

Senior linebacker Sione Fotu missed most of practice last week to be with his wife as the couple welcomed their first child, a daughter, on Friday.

Defensive lineman Quindario Lee said the baby now has "104 uncles on the team."

Fotu played sparingly in Saturday's game but was back at practice full-time Monday.

Fritz joked that he "compared notes with having little girls."

"That was a long time ago for me," said Fritz, who has two daughters and a son.

Fritz said he was happy for former UH star wide receiver Tank Dell, who scored his first touchdown in two years for the Texans on Sunday.

Dell caught a 63-yard touchdown in the first half of the Texans' 44-22 win over the Chargers, his first touchdown since suffering a devastating knee injury in 2024.

"We're just so happy for him," Fritz said. "Tank has responded the best way he can to adversity. He's up here a lot. I've gotten to know him fairly well. He loves the University of Houston, and the University of Houston loves him."

Dell attended the Cougars' Oct. 17 game against Oklahoma State and was shown on the video board at TDECU Stadium.

"Everybody was going crazy over it," Fritz said.

ESPN and Fox will exercise a six-day selection window to determine the kickoff time for UH's Nov. 21 game at West Virginia, the Big 12 announced Monday.

This article originally published at Willie Fritz on expanded College Football Playoff and Cougars' chances this year | UH football notes.

#2715. Warriors guard Will Richard out indefinitely with ligament tear in thumb

The Golden State Warriors announced some surprising news on the team's injury report for Monday night's game against the New Orleans Pelicans. Per the Dubs official release, second-year guard Will Richard is out indefinitely with a torn ulnar collateral ligament (UCL) in his left thumb. A 2023 study found that NBA players who suffered in-season thumb UCL tears missed on average 7.4 weeks of action (although there was a wide range of outcomes). Kings forward Keegan Murray suffered a similar injury last year and missed the first 15 games of the season.

Whether Richard is out for a month, three, or more, head coach Steve Kerr will now have a fourth player on the roster unavailable to him in the rotation. Richard joins Jimmy Butler, Moses Moody, and Kristaps Porzingis on the Warriors list of players with prolonged absences. Richard was off to a promising start to his sophomore NBA season prior to the injury, averaging 5.1 points and 1.8 rebounds on 47.5%/42.9%/80.0% shooting in 17.2 minutes per game across 10 games (3 starts).

The Warriors acquired Richard on a draft night trade back in 2025, acquiring his rights via the 56th overall pick. Richard had helped Florida win a National Championship in his final collegiate season and quickly made an impression in Golden State, looking like an early steal. Richard exploded for 30 points in his seventh NBA game and quickly became a mainstay in the rotation. However, he never again reached those heights and became a solid role player whose effectiveness ebbed and flowed with his jump shot.

Thus far this season, Richard was making his threes, but he was also continuing to show veteran savvy as a backcutter in the Warriors offense. Moreover, he was one of Kerr's go-to point of attack defenders against quick guards. Golden State already lacked point of attack defense, and the current roster does not have any obvious replacements for that component of Richard's role. Gary Payton II will likely receive more opportunities with perhaps a few minutes for two-way wing Malevy Leons.

The Russian said he decided this summer that the 2026-27 campaign would be his last, but that he purposefully waited until ahead of the home opener: "It was important for me to make this announcement before our first home game, so I have the chance to thank all the people around the game, everyone who has been so important to me and my family and helped me through the good times and tough losses," Ovechkin wrote. "I wouldn't have been able to achieve everything I have in my life without them. I am excited about this season with my teammates, and we will do everything we can to make this year special."

In July, Ovechkin signed a one-year deal with the Caps worth $4.25 million – he gets a whopping $4.75 million performance bonus when he plays in his 10th game -- after playing in all 82 last year and putting up yet another 30-goal season with 32. It was his NHL-record 20th 30-goal season. But his average ice time dipped to a career-low 17:27. Through two games this year, Ovechkin is averaging only 13:10 of ice time and has an assist.

Ovi ranks No. 1 in league history in the regular season in goals (929), power-play goals (331), game-winning goals (141), empty-net goals (72) and overtime goals (27). But if we include playoffs, Ovechkin's 1,006 goals are second behind Wayne Gretzky's 1,016. You know Ovechkin wants that mark too and barring injury should get it. If he scores 20 goals this season, he'll tie Gordie Howe for the most 20-goal campaigns at 22.

He's +225 to get one tonight. Ovechkin has scored on 189 different goalies during his NHL career but not yet against Pittsburgh's 25-year-old Arturs Silovs (2-0-1, 2.46 GAA). Silovs was a backup in Vancouver his first three NHL seasons and only got regular time last season with Pittsburgh, so Ovechkin has only faced him twice. Silovs allowed eight goals in two starts last year vs. Washington, splitting them. Ovi did score in one of them, but on an empty net.
